\displaystyle c_{\nu}\,n^{\min\{0,\nu/2-1\}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|Y_{i}|^{\nu}]\;\leq\;\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\msum_{i=1}^{n}Y_{i}\big|^{\nu}\big]\;\leq\;C_{\nu}\,n^{\max\{0,\nu/2-1\}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|Y_{i}|^{\nu}]\;,

for
C_{\nu}:=\max\big\{2,(8(\nu-1)\max\{1,2^{\nu-3}\})^{\nu}\big\}
and some absolute constant
c_{\nu}>0
that depends only on
\nu
.

The next moment computation for a quadratic form of Gaussians is used throughout the proof:

Lemma 27

(Lemma 2.3,
Magnus 1978
)
.

Given a standard Gaussian vector
\eta
in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
and a symmetric
m\times m
matrix
A
, we have that
\mathbb{E}[\eta^{\top}A\eta]=\text{\rm Tr}(A)
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\eta^{\top}A\eta)^{2}]\;=\;\text{\rm Tr}(A)^{2}+2\text{\rm Tr}(A^{2})\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\eta^{\top}A\eta)^{3}]\;=\;\text{\rm Tr}(A)^{3}+6\text{\rm Tr}(A)\text{\rm Tr}(A^{2})+8\text{\rm Tr}(A^{3})\;.

The next two results are used for the moment computation involving an RBF kernel.

Lemma 28
.

Fix
\mathbf{m}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
a_{i}>0
for
i=1,2
. Let
\mathbf{X}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{m}_{1},a_{1}^{2}I_{d})
, and let
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be a deterministic function such that
\mathbb{E}[|f(\mathbf{X})|]<\infty
. It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[f(\mathbf{X})\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bX- \bm_2 \|_2^2 }{2a_2^2}$}}\Big)\Big]\;

\displaystyle=\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{a_2^2}{a_1^2 + a_2^2}$}}\Big)^{d/2}\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \| \bm_1 - \bm_2 \|_2^2}{2(a_1^2 + a_2^2)}$}}\Big)\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{W})]\;,

where
\mathbf{W}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{m},a^{2}I_{d})
with
\,\mathbf{m}\;\coloneqq\;\frac{a_{1}^{2}a_{2}^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}\big(\frac{1}{a_{1}^{2}}\mathbf{m}_{1}+\frac{1}{a_{2}^{2}}\mathbf{m}_{2}\big)

and

a^{2}\coloneqq\frac{a_{1}^{2}a_{2}^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}}
.

Lemma 29
.

Fix
\mathbf{m}_{1},\mathbf{m}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
a_{i}>0
for
i=1,2,3
. Let
\mathbf{X}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{m}_{1},a_{1}^{2}I_{d})
and
\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{m}_{2},a_{2}^{2}I_{d})
, and let
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be a deterministic function with
\mathbb{E}[|f(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{X}^{\prime})|]<\infty
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[f(\mathbf{X},\mathbf{X}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \| \bX- \bX' \|_2^2}{2a_3^2}$}}\right)\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{a_3^2}{a_1^2 + a_2^2 + a_3^2}$}}\Big)^{d/2}\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \| \bm_1 - \bm_2 \|_2^2}{2(a_1^2 + a_2^2 + a_3^2)}$}}\Big)\mathbb{E}\Big[f\Big(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{W}^{\prime}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{a_2^2 }{a_2^2+a_3^2}$}}\mathbf{W}\Big)\Big]\;,

where
\mathbf{W}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\mathbf{m},a^{2}I_{d}\right)
and
\mathbf{W}^{\prime}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\mathbf{m}^{\prime},(a^{\prime})^{2}I_{d}\right)
are independent with

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{a_1^2(a_2^2 + a_3^2)}{a_1^2 + a_2^2 + a_3^2}$}}\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{a_1^2}$}}\mathbf{m}_{1}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{a_2^2 + a_3^2}$}}\mathbf{m}_{2}\right)\;,

\displaystyle\quad a^{2}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{a_1^2(a_2^2 + a_3^2)}{a_1^2 + a_2^2 + a_3^2}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{a_3^2}{a_2^2 + a_3^2}$}}\mathbf{m}_{2}\;,

\displaystyle(a^{\prime})^{2}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{a_2^2 a_3^2}{a_2^2 + a_3^2}$}}\;.

B.2
Moment bounds for U-statistics

We first present a result that bounds the moments of a U-statistic
D_{n}
defined as in (
1
).

Lemma 30
.

Fix
n\geq 2
and
\nu\geq 2
. Then, there exist absolute constants
c_{\nu},C_{\nu}>0
depending only on
\nu
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|D_{n}-\mathbb{E}D_{n}|^{\nu}]\;

\displaystyle\leq\;C_{\nu}\,n^{\nu/2}(n-1)^{-\nu}M_{\rm cond;\nu}^{\nu}+C_{\nu}\,(n-1)^{-\nu}M_{\rm full;\nu}^{\nu}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|D_{n}-\mathbb{E}D_{n}|^{\nu}]\;

\displaystyle\geq\;c_{\nu}n(n-1)^{-\nu}M_{\rm cond;\nu}^{\nu}+c_{\nu}n^{-(\nu-1)}(n-1)^{-(\nu-1)}M_{\rm full;\nu}^{\nu}\;.

In other words,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|D_{n}-\mathbb{E}D_{n}|^{\nu}]\;

\displaystyle=\;O(n^{-\nu/2}M_{\rm cond;\nu}^{\nu}+n^{-\nu}M_{\rm full;\nu}^{\nu})\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|D_{n}-\mathbb{E}D_{n}|^{\nu}]\;

\displaystyle=\;\Omega(n^{-(\nu-1)}M_{\rm cond;\nu}^{\nu}+n^{-2(\nu-1)}M_{\rm full;\nu}^{\nu})\;.

The next two results summarise how the moments of variables under the functional decomposition in
2
interact with the moments of the original statistic
u
under
R
:

Lemma 31
.

Let
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
,
\{\lambda_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
and
\varepsilon_{K;\nu}
be defined as in
2
. For
\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2}\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}R
, write
\mu_{k}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})]
and let the moment terms
D,M_{\rm cond;\nu},M_{\rm full;\nu}
be defined as in
Section

2.1
. Then we have the following:

(i)

\big|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\mu_{k}^{2}-D\big|\leq\varepsilon_{K;1}
;

(ii)

for any
\nu\in[1,3]
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4}$}}(M_{\rm cond;\nu})^{\nu}-\varepsilon_{K;\nu}^{\nu}\;\leq\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}(\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})-\mu_{k})\mu_{k}\Big|^{\nu}\Big]\;\leq\;4((M_{\rm cond;\nu})^{\nu}+\varepsilon_{K;\nu}^{\nu})\;;

(iii)

there exist some absolute constants
c,C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}(\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})-\mu_{k})(\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})-\mu_{k})\Big|^{\nu}\Big]\leq

\displaystyle\;4C(M_{\rm full;\nu})^{\nu}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}(M_{\rm cond;\nu})^{\nu}+(4C+2)\varepsilon_{K;\nu}^{\nu}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}(\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})-\mu_{k})(\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})-\mu_{k})\Big|^{\nu}\Big]\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{c}{4}$}}(M_{\rm full;\nu})^{\nu}-8(M_{\rm cond;\nu})^{\nu}-(c+8)\varepsilon_{K;\nu}^{\nu}\;.

The next result assumes the notations of Lemma
31
, and additionally denotes

\displaystyle\Lambda^{K}\;\coloneqq\;\text{\rm diag}\{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{K}\}\;\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}\;,

\displaystyle\phi^{K}(x)\;\coloneqq\;(\phi_{1}(x),\ldots,\phi_{K}(x))^{\top}\;\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\;.

Lemma 32
.

For
\mu^{K}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{1})]
and

\Sigma^{K}\coloneqq\text{\rm Cov}[\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{1})]
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}-4\sigma_{\rm cond}\varepsilon_{K;2}-4\varepsilon_{K;2}^{2}\;\leq\;

\displaystyle(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K}(\mu^{K})\;\leq\;(\sigma_{\rm cond}+2\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}\;.

\displaystyle(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}\;\leq\;

\displaystyle\text{\rm Tr}((\Lambda^{K}\Sigma^{K})^{2})\;\leq\;(\sigma_{\rm full}+\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}\;.

In particular, for
\nu\in[1,3]
and two i.i.d. zero-mean Gaussian vector
\mathbf{Z}_{1}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{2}
in
\mathbb{R}^{K}
with variance
\Sigma^{K}
, there exists some absolute constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\mathbf{Z}_{1}|^{\nu}]\;\leq\;7\big(\sigma_{\rm cond}^{\nu}+8\varepsilon_{K;2}^{\nu}\big)\;,\qquad\mathbb{E}[|\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\mathbf{Z}_{2}|^{\nu}]\;\leq\;6\big(\sigma_{\rm full}^{\nu}+\varepsilon_{K;2}^{\nu}\big)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|(\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{1})-\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\big|^{\nu}\big]\;\leq\;8C(M_{\rm full;\nu})^{\nu}-(M_{\rm cond;\nu})^{\nu}+(8C+4)\varepsilon_{K;\nu}^{\nu}\;.

The next lemma gives an equivalent expression for
W_{n}^{K}
defined in (
5
) and also controls the moments of
W_{n}^{K}
.

Lemma 33
.

Let
\{\eta^{K}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be a sequence of i.i.d. standard Gaussian vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{K}
. Then

(i)

the distribution of
W_{n}^{K}
satisfies

\displaystyle W_{n}^{K}\;\overset{d}{=}\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^{3/2}(n-1)^{1/2}}$}}\Big(\msum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\eta^{K}_{i})^{\top}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{j}-n\text{\rm Tr}(\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K})\Big)+D\;;

(ii)

the mean satisfies
\mathbb{E}[W_{n}^{K}]=D
for every
K\in\mathbb{N}
;

(iii)

the variance is controlled as

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n(n-1)}$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\text{\rm Var}[W_{n}^{K}]\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n(n-1)}$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}+\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}\;;

(iv)

the third central moment is controlled as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K}-D)^{3}\big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{8 \big( \mean[u(\bX_1,\bX_2)u(\bX_2,\bX_3)u(\bX_3,\bX_1)] - \Mfullthree^3 + ( \Mfullthree+ \varepsilon_{K;3})^3 \big)}{n^{3/2}(n-1)^{3/2}}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K}-D)^{3}\big]\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{8 \big( \mean[u(\bX_1,\bX_2)u(\bX_2,\bX_3)u(\bX_3,\bX_1)] + \Mfullthree^3 - ( \Mfullthree+ \varepsilon_{K;3})^3 \big)}{n^{3/2}(n-1)^{3/2}}$}}\;;

(v)

the fourth central moment is controlled as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K}-D)^{4}\big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{12}{n^2(n-1)^2}$}}\Big(4\,\mathbb{E}[u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})u(\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{3})u(\mathbf{X}_{3},\mathbf{X}_{4})u(\mathbf{X}_{4},\mathbf{X}_{1})]

\displaystyle\;\qquad\qquad\quad-4M_{\rm full;4}^{4}+4(M_{\rm full;4}+\varepsilon_{K;4})^{4}+(\sigma_{\rm full}+\varepsilon_{K;2})^{4}\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K}-D)^{4}\big]\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{12}{n^2(n-1)^2}$}}\Big(4\,\mathbb{E}[u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})u(\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{3})u(\mathbf{X}_{3},\mathbf{X}_{4})u(\mathbf{X}_{4},\mathbf{X}_{1})]

\displaystyle\;\qquad\qquad\quad+4M_{\rm full;4}^{4}-4(M_{\rm full;4}+\varepsilon_{K;4})^{4}+(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{4}\Big)\;;

(vi)

we also have a generic moment bound: For
m\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists some absolute constant
C_{m}>0
depending only on
m
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K})^{2m}\big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C_m}{n^{m}(n-1)^{m} }$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}+\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2m}+C_{m}\,D^{2m}\;;

(vii)

if
2
holds for some
\nu\geq 2
then
\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\text{\rm Var}[W_{n}^{K}]=\frac{2}{n(n-1)}\sigma_{\rm full}^{2}
. If
2
holds for some
\nu\geq 3
, then

\displaystyle\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K}-D)^{3}\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{8 \mean[u(\bX_1,\bX_2)u(\bX_2,\bX_3)u(\bX_3,\bX_1)]}{n^{3/2}(n-1)^{3/2}}$}}\;,

and if
2
holds for some
\nu\geq 4
, then

\displaystyle\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K}-D)^{4}\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{12 (4 \mean[u(\bX_1,\bX_2)u(\bX_2,\bX_3)u(\bX_3,\bX_4)u(\bX_4,\bX_1)] + \sigfull^4)}{n^2(n-1)^2}$}}\;.

B.3
Distribution bounds

The following is a standard approximation of an indicator function for bounding the probability of a given event; see e.g. the proof of Theorem 3.3,
Chen et al. 2011
.

Lemma 34
.

Fix any
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and
\tau,\delta\in\mathbb{R}
. Then there exists an
m
-times differentiable
\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
function
h_{m;\tau;\delta}
such that
h_{m;\tau+\delta;\delta}(x)\;\leq\;\mathbb{I}_{\{x>\tau\}}\leq h_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)
. For
0\leq r\leq m
, the
r
-th derivative
h^{(r)}_{m;\tau;\delta}
is continuous and bounded above by
\delta^{-r}
. Moreover, for every
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,
h^{(m)}
satisfies that

\displaystyle|h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)-h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(y)|\;\leq\;C_{m,\epsilon}\,\delta^{-(m+\epsilon)}\,|x-y|^{\epsilon}\;,

with respect to the constant
C_{m,\epsilon}=\binom{m}{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}(m+1)^{m+\epsilon}
.

The next bound is useful for approximating the distribution of a sum of two (possibly correlated) random variables
X
and
Y
by the distribution of
X
alone, provided that the influence of
Y
is small.

Lemma 35
.

For two real-valued random variables
X
and
Y
, any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
\epsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq X+Y\leq b)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}(a-\epsilon\leq X\leq b+\epsilon)+\mathbb{P}(|Y|\geq\epsilon)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq X+Y\leq b)\;\geq\;\mathbb{P}(a+\epsilon\leq X\leq b-\epsilon)-\mathbb{P}(|Y|\geq\epsilon)\;.

Theorem 8 of
Carbery and Wright 2001
gives a general anti-concentration result for a polynomial of random variables drawn from a log-concave density. The next lemma restates the result in the case of a quadratic form of a
K
-dimensional standard Gaussian vector
\eta
.

Lemma 36
.

Let
p(\mathbf{x})
be a degree-two polynomial of
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
taking values in
\mathbb{R}
. Then there exists an absolute constant
C
independent of
p
and
\eta
such that, for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|p(\eta)|\leq t\big)\;\leq\;Ct^{1/2}(\mathbb{E}[|p(\eta)|^{2}])^{-1/4}\;\leq\;Ct^{1/2}(\text{\rm Var}[p(\eta)])^{-1/4}\;.

B.4
Weak Mercer representation

In
Section

4.2
, we have used the
weak Mercer representation
from
Steinwart and Scovel 2012
. We summarise their result below, which combines their Lemma 2.3, Lemma 2.12 and Corollary 3.2:

Lemma 37
.

Consider a probability measure
R
on
\mathbb{R}^{b}
,
\mathbf{V}_{1},\mathbf{V}_{2}\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}R
and a measurable kernel
\kappa^{*}
on
\mathbb{R}^{b}
. If
\mathbb{E}[\kappa^{*}(\mathbf{V}_{1},\mathbf{V}_{1})]<\infty
, there exists a sequence of functions
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
in
L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{b},R)
and a bounded sequence of non-negative values
\{\lambda_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
with
\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\lambda_{k}=0
, such that as
K
grows,

\big|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{V}_{1})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{V}_{2})-\kappa^{*}(\mathbf{V}_{1},\mathbf{V}_{2})\big|\rightarrow 0
. The series converges
R\otimes R
almost surely.

Appendix C
Proof of the main result

In this section, we prove
Theorem

2
. The proof is necessarily tedious as we seek to control “spectral” approximation errors (i.e. the error from a truncated functional decomposition) and multiple stochastic approximation errors at the same time. The section is organised as follows:

•

In
Section

C.1
, we list notations and key lemmas that formalise the steps in the proof outline in
Section

3.3
;

•

In
Section

C.2
, we present the proof body of
Theorem

2
, which directly combines results from the different lemmas;

•

In
Section

C.3
,
C.4
,
Section

C.5
and
C.6
, we present the proof of the key lemmas. Each section starts with an informal sketch of proof ideas followed by the actual proof of the result.

C.1
Auxiliary lemmas

Recall that our goal is to study the distribution of

\displaystyle D_{n}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)}$}}\msum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}u(\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{X}_{j})\;.

The three results in this section form the key steps of the proof. We fix
\sigma>0
to be some normalisation constant to be chosen later.

1. “Spectral” approximation.
For
K\in\mathbb{N}
, we define the truncated version of
D_{n}
by

\displaystyle D^{K}_{n}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)}$}}\msum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{i})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{j})

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)}$}}\msum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}(\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{i}))^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{j})\;.

We also denote the rescaled statistics for convenience as

\displaystyle\tilde{D}_{n}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}\,D_{n}\;,

\displaystyle\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}\,D_{n}^{K}\;.

The first lemma allows us to study the distribution of
D_{n}^{K}
in lieu of that of
D_{n}
up to some approximation error that vanishes as
K
grows.

Lemma 38
.

Fix
\delta,\sigma>0
,
K\in\mathbb{N}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t+\delta)-\varepsilon_{K}^{\prime}\leq\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}>t)\leq\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t-\delta)+\varepsilon_{K}^{\prime}\;,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3 n^{1/4}(n-1)^{1/4} \varepsilon_{K;1}^{1/2}}{\sigma^{1/2} \delta^{1/2} }$}}\;.

2. Gaussian approximation via Lindeberg’s technique.
The distribution of
D_{n}^{K}
is easier to handle, as it is a double sum of a simple quadratic form of
K
-dimensional random vectors.
Let
\mathbf{Z}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{Z}_{n}
be i.i.d. Gaussian random vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{K}
with mean and variance matching those of
\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{1})
, and denote
Z_{ik}
as the
k
-th coordinate of
\mathbf{Z}_{i}
.
The goal is to replace
D_{n}^{K}
by the random variable

\displaystyle D^{K}_{Z}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)}$}}\msum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}\mathbf{Z}_{i}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\mathbf{Z}_{j}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)}$}}\msum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}Z_{ik}Z_{jk}\;.

Notice that
D^{K}_{Z}
takes the same form as
D_{n}^{K}
except that each
\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{i})
is replaced by
\mathbf{Z}_{i}
. Analogous to
\tilde{D}_{n}
and
\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}
, we also define a rescaled version as

\displaystyle\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}\, D_Z^K}{\sigma}$}}\;.

The second lemma replaces the distribution
\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}
by that of
\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}
, up to some approximation error that vanishes as
n
grows:

Lemma 39
.

Fix
\delta,\sigma>0
,
K\in\mathbb{N}
,
t\in\mathbb{R}
and any
\nu\in(2,3]
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t-\delta)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t-2\delta)+E_{\delta;K}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t+\delta)\;\geq\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t+2\delta)-E_{\delta;K}\;,

where the approximation error is defined as, for some absolute constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle E_{\delta;K}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C}{\delta^\nu n^{\nu/2-1}}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mfullnu)^\nu+
\varepsilon_{K;\nu}^\nu}{\sigma^{\nu} }$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mcondnu)^\nu+ \varepsilon_{K;\nu}^{\nu} }{(n-1)^{-\nu/2}\,\sigma^{\nu}}$}}\Big)\;.

3. Replace
D^{K}_{Z}
by
U^{K}_{n}
.
As in the statement of
Theorem

2
, let
\{\eta^{K}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be the i.i.d. standard normal vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{K}
, and recall the notations
\mu^{K}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{1})]
and
\Sigma^{K}\coloneqq\text{\rm Cov}[\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{1})]
. We can then express
D^{K}_{Z}
as

\displaystyle D^{K}_{Z}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)}$}}\msum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}\big((\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{i}+\mu^{K}\big)^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\big((\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{j}+\mu^{K}\big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)}$}}\msum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}(\eta^{K}_{i})^{\top}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{j}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{i}

\displaystyle\;+(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\mu^{K}\;.

This is similar to the desired variable
U_{n}^{K}
except for the third term:

\displaystyle U_{n}^{K}=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)}$}}\msum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}(\eta^{K}_{i})^{\top}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{j}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{i}+D\;.

As before, we denote
\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\coloneqq\frac{\sqrt{n(n-1)}U_{n}^{K}}{\sigma}
. The next lemma shows that the distribution of
\tilde{D}^{K}_{Z}
can be approximated by that of
\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}
, up to some approximation error that vanishes as
K\rightarrow\infty
.

Lemma 40
.

For any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
\epsilon>0
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}\leq b)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\;\mathbb{P}\big(a-\epsilon\leq\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\leq b+\epsilon\big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \varepsilon_{K;1}}{ \epsilon\, n^{-1/2} (n-1)^{-1/2} \sigma}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}\leq b)\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\;\mathbb{P}(a+\epsilon\leq\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\leq b-\epsilon)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \varepsilon_{K;1}}{ \epsilon\, n^{-1/2} (n-1)^{-1/2} \sigma}$}}\;.

4. Bound the distribution of
\tilde{U}^{K}_{n}
over a short interval.
If we are to use Lemma
38
and Lemma
39
directly, we would end up comparing
\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}>t)
against the probabilities
\mathbb{P}(\tilde{U}^{K}_{n}>t+2\delta)
and
\mathbb{P}(\tilde{U}^{K}_{n}>t-2\delta)
for some small
\delta>0
. It turns out these are not too different from
\mathbb{P}(\tilde{U}^{K}_{n}>t)
: As
\tilde{U}^{K}_{n}
is a quadratic form of Gaussians, we can ensure it is “well spread-out” such that the probability mass of
\tilde{U}^{K}_{n}
within a small interval
(t-2\delta,t+2\delta)
is not too large. This is ascertained by the following lemma:

Lemma 41
.

For
a\leq b\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists some absolute constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\leq b)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C(b-a)^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sigma^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n-1}{\sigma^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}-2\sigma_{\rm cond}\varepsilon_{K;2}-4\varepsilon_{K;2})\Big)^{-1/4}\;.

C.2
Proof body of
Theorem

2

Fix
\delta,\sigma>0
,
K\in\mathbb{N}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
. By Lemma
38
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t+\delta)-\varepsilon_{K}^{\prime}\leq\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}>t)\leq\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t-\delta)+\varepsilon_{K}^{\prime}\;,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3 n^{1/4}(n-1)^{1/4} \varepsilon_{K;1}^{1/2}}{\sigma^{1/2} \delta^{1/2} }$}}\;.

By Lemma
39
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t-\delta)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t-2\delta)+E_{\delta;K}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t+\delta)\;\geq\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t+2\delta)-E_{\delta;K}\;,

where the error term is defined as, for some absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
,

\displaystyle E_{\delta;K}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C' }{\delta^\nu n^{\nu/2-1}}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mfullnu)^\nu+
\varepsilon_{K;\nu}^\nu}{\sigma^{\nu} }$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mcondnu)^\nu+ \varepsilon_{K;\nu}^{\nu} }{(n-1)^{-\nu/2}\,\sigma^{\nu}}$}}\Big)\;.

To combine the two bounds, we consider the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t-2\delta)\;

\displaystyle=\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t)+\mathbb{P}(t-2\delta<\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}\leq t)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t+2\delta)\;

\displaystyle=\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t)-\mathbb{P}(t<\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}\leq t+2\delta)\;.

(12)

This allows us to combine the earlier two bounds as

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}>t)-\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t)\big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\max\nolimits\{\mathbb{P}(t-2\delta\leq\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}<t)\;,\mathbb{P}(t<\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}\leq t+2\delta)\}+E_{\delta;K}+\varepsilon^{\prime}_{K}\;,

which gives the error of approximating the c.d.f. of
\tilde{D}_{n}
by that of
\tilde{D}^{K}_{Z}
. Now fix some
\epsilon>0
. By applying
Lemma
40
and taking appropriate limits of the endpoints to change
\leq
to
<
,
\geq
to
>
and taking the right endpoint to positive infinity, we can now approximate the c.d.f. of
\tilde{D}^{K}_{Z}
by that of
\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(t-2\delta\leq\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}<t)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\;\mathbb{P}\big(t-2\delta-\epsilon\leq\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}<t+\epsilon\big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \varepsilon_{K;1}}{ \epsilon\, n^{-1/2} (n-1)^{-1/2} \sigma}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(t\leq\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}<t+2\delta)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\;\mathbb{P}(t-\epsilon\leq\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}<t+2\delta+\epsilon)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \varepsilon_{K;1}}{ \epsilon\, n^{-1/2} (n-1)^{-1/2} \sigma}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\;\mathbb{P}\big(\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}>t-\epsilon\big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \varepsilon_{K;1}}{ \epsilon\, n^{-1/2} (n-1)^{-1/2} \sigma}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t)\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}>t+\epsilon)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \varepsilon_{K;1}}{ \epsilon\, n^{-1/2} (n-1)^{-1/2} \sigma}$}}\;.

Substituting the bounds into the earlier bound and using a similar decomposition to (
12
), we get that the error of approximating the c.d.f. of
\tilde{D}_{n}
by that of
\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}
is

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}>t)-\mathbb{P}(\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}>t)\big|\;

\displaystyle\leq\;\max\nolimits\{\mathbb{P}(t-\epsilon\leq\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}<t)\;,\mathbb{P}(t<\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\leq t+\epsilon)\}

\displaystyle\;+\max\nolimits\{\mathbb{P}(t-2\delta-\epsilon\leq\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}<t+\epsilon)\;,\mathbb{P}(t-\epsilon<\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\leq t+2\delta+\epsilon)\}

\displaystyle\;+E_{\delta;K}+\varepsilon^{\prime}_{K}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ 4 \varepsilon_{K;1}}{ \epsilon\, n^{-1/2} (n-1)^{-1/2} \sigma}$}}\;.

To bound the maxima, we recall that by Lemma
41
, there exists some absolute constant
C^{\prime\prime}
such that for any
a\leq b\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\leq b)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime\prime}(b-a)^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sigma^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n-1}{\sigma^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}-2\sigma_{\rm cond}\varepsilon_{K;2}-4\varepsilon)\Big)^{-1/4}\;.

Substituting this into the above bound while noting
(2\delta+2\epsilon)^{1/2}\leq 2\delta^{1/2}+2\epsilon^{1/2}
, we get that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}

\displaystyle>t)-\mathbb{P}(\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}>t)\big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime\prime}\big(6\epsilon^{1/2}+4\delta^{1/2}\big)\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sigma^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n-1}{\sigma^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}-2\sigma_{\rm cond}\varepsilon_{K;2}-4\varepsilon_{K;2})\Big)^{-1/4}

\displaystyle\;+E_{\delta;K}+\varepsilon^{\prime}_{K}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ 4 \varepsilon_{K;1}}{ \epsilon\, n^{-1/2} (n-1)^{-1/2} \sigma}$}}\;.

We now take
K\rightarrow\infty
. By
2
,
\varepsilon_{K;2}\rightarrow 0
in the first term and the two trailing error terms vanish. The second error term becomes

\displaystyle E_{\delta;K}\;\rightarrow\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C'}{\delta^\nu n^{\nu/2-1}}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mfullnu)^\nu}{\sigma^{\nu} }$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mcondnu)^\nu}{(n-1)^{-\nu/2}\,\sigma^{\nu}}$}}\Big)\;.

By additionally taking
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
in the first term and taking a supremum over
t
on both sides, we then obtain

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}>t)-\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}>t)\Big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4C^{\prime\prime}\delta^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigfull^2}{\sigma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigcond^2}{(n-1)^{-1}\sigma^2}$}}\Big)^{-1/4}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C'}{\delta^\nu n^{\nu/2-1}}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mfullnu)^\nu}{\sigma^{\nu} }$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mcondnu)^\nu}{(n-1)^{-\nu/2}\,\sigma^{\nu}}$}}\Big)\;.

Finally, we choose

\displaystyle\delta\;=\;n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{2\nu+1}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mfullnu)^\nu}{\sigma^{\nu} }$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mcondnu)^\nu}{(n-1)^{-\nu/2}\,\sigma^{\nu}}$}}\Big)^{\frac{2}{2\nu+1}}

and
\sigma=\sigma_{\rm max}\coloneqq\max\{\sigma_{\rm full},(n-1)^{1/2}\sigma_{\rm cond}\}
. Then
\big(\frac{\sigma_{\rm full}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+\frac{\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}}{(n-1)^{-1}\sigma^{2}}\big)^{-1/4}\leq 1
, and by redefining constants, we get that there exists some absolute constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\;\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigmax}$}}D_{n}>t\Big)-\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigmax}$}}U_{n}^{K}>t\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\;C\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{4\nu+2}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mfullnu)^\nu}{\sigmax^{\nu} }$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mcondnu)^\nu}{(n-1)^{-\nu/2}\,\sigmax^{\nu}}$}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2\nu+1}}

(13)

\displaystyle\leq\;2^{\frac{1}{2\nu+1}}C\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{4\nu+2}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\Mmaxnu}{\sigmax}$}}\Big)^{\frac{\nu}{2\nu+1}}\;,

where we have recalled that
M_{\rm max;\nu}\coloneqq\max\{M_{\rm full;\nu},(n-1)^{1/2}M_{\rm cond;\nu}\}
. This finishes the proof.

C.3
Proof of Lemma
38

Proof overview.
The proof idea is reminiscent of the standard technique for proving that convergence in probability implies weak convergence. We first approximate each probability by the expectation of a
\delta^{-1}
Lipschitz function
h
that is uniformly bounded by
1
. This introduces an approximation error of
\delta
, while replaces the difference in probability by the difference
\mathbb{E}[h(\tilde{D}_{n})-h(\tilde{D}^{K}_{n})]
. The expectation can be further split by the events
\{|\tilde{D}_{n}-\tilde{D}^{K}_{n}|<\epsilon\}
and
\{|\tilde{D}_{n}-\tilde{D}^{K}_{n}|\geq\epsilon\}
. In the first case, the expectation can be bounded by a Lipschitz argument; in the second case, we can use the boundedness of
h
to bound the expectation by
2\mathbb{P}(|\tilde{D}_{n}-\tilde{D}^{K}_{n}|\geq\epsilon)
, which is in turn bounded by a Markov argument to give the “spectral” approximation error. Choosing
\epsilon
appropriately gives the above error term.

Proof of Lemma
38

For any
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
and
\delta>0
, let
h_{\tau;\delta}
be the function defined in Lemma
34
with
m=0
, which satisfies

h_{\tau+\delta;\delta}(x)\;\leq\;\mathbb{I}_{\{x>\tau\}}\;\leq\;h_{\tau;\delta}(x)\;.

By applying the above bounds with
\tau
set to
t
and
t-\delta
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}>t)-\mathbb{P}\big(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t-\delta\big)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{I}_{\{\tilde{D}_{n}>t\}}-\mathbb{I}_{\{\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t-\delta\}}]\;\leq\;\mathbb{E}[h_{t;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{n})-h_{t;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K})]\;,

and similarly

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t+\delta\big)-\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}>t)\;\leq\;\mathbb{E}[h_{t+\delta;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K})-h_{t+\delta;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{n})]\;.

Therefore, defining
\xi_{\tau}:=|\mathbb{E}[h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{n})-h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K})]|
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t+\delta\big)-\xi_{t+\delta}\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}>t)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}\big(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t-\delta\big)+\xi_{t}\;.

To bound quantities of the form
\xi_{\tau}
, fix any
\epsilon>0
and write
\xi_{\tau}=\xi_{\tau,1}+\xi_{\tau,2}
where

\displaystyle\xi_{\tau,1}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{n})-h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K})\big)\mathbb{I}_{\{|\tilde{D}_{n}-\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}|\leq\epsilon\}}\Big]\Big|\;,

\displaystyle\xi_{\tau,2}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{n})-h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K})\big)\mathbb{I}_{\{|\tilde{D}_{n}-\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}|>\epsilon\}}\Big]\Big|\;.

The first term can be bounded by recalling from Lemma
34
that
h_{\tau;\delta}
is
\delta^{-1}
-Lipschitz:

\displaystyle\xi_{\tau,1}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\delta^{-1}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\tilde{D}_{n}-\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}\big|\mathbb{I}_{\{|\tilde{D}_{n}-\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}|\leq\epsilon\}}\big]\;\leq\;\delta^{-1}\epsilon\,\mathbb{P}\big(|\tilde{D}_{n}-\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}|\leq\epsilon\big)\;\leq\;\delta^{-1}\epsilon\;.

The second term can be bounded by noting that
h_{\tau;\delta}
is uniformly bounded above by
1
and applying Markov’s inequality:

\displaystyle\xi_{\tau,2}\;\leq\;2\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{I}_{\{|\tilde{D}_{n}-\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}|>\epsilon\}}]\;=\;2\mathbb{P}(|\tilde{D}_{n}-\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}|>\epsilon)\;\leq\;2\epsilon^{-1}\mathbb{E}\big[|\tilde{D}_{n}-\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}|\big]\;.

By the definition of
\tilde{D}_{n}
and
\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}
, a triangle inequality and noting that
\mathbf{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{X}_{n}
are i.i.d. , the absolute moment term can be bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\tilde{D}_{n}-\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}|\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}\,\mathbb{E}\big[|D_{n}-D_{n}^{K}|\big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n(n-1)}}$}}\Big\|\msum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}\big(u(\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{X}_{j})-\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{i})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{j})\big)\Big\|_{L_{1}}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}\Big\|u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})-\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\Big\|_{L_{1}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\sigma^{-1}\sqrt{n(n-1)}\,\varepsilon_{K;1}\;.

Combining the bounds on
\xi_{\tau,1},\xi_{\tau,2}
and
\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{D}_{n}-\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}|]
and choosing
\epsilon=\big(\sqrt{n(n-1)}\sigma^{-1}\delta\varepsilon_{K;1})^{1/2}
, we get that

\displaystyle\xi_{\tau}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\delta^{-1}\epsilon+2\sqrt{n(n-1)}\,\epsilon^{-1}\sigma^{-1}\varepsilon_{K;1}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3 n^{1/4}(n-1)^{1/4} \varepsilon_{K;1}^{1/2}}{\sigma^{1/2} \delta^{1/2} }$}}\;\eqqcolon\;\varepsilon_{K}^{\prime}\;,

which yields the desired bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t+\delta)-\varepsilon_{K}^{\prime}\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}>t)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t-\delta)+\varepsilon_{K}^{\prime}\;.

\jmlrQED

C.4
Proof of Lemma
39

For convenience, we denote
\mathbf{V}_{i}\coloneqq\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{i})
throughout this section.

Proof overview.
The key idea in the proof rests on Lindeberg’s telescoping sum argument for central limit theorem. We follow
Chatterjee 2006
’s adaptataion of the Lindeberg idea for statistics that are not asymptotically normal. As before, the difference in probability is first approximated by a difference in expectation
\mathbb{E}[h(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K})-h(\tilde{D}^{K}_{Z})]
with respect to some function
h
, which introduces a further approximation error
\delta
. The next step is to note that both
\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}
and
\tilde{D}^{K}_{Z}
can be expressed in terms of some common function
\tilde{f}
, such that

\displaystyle\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}\;=\;\tilde{f}(\mathbf{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{V}_{n})\;,

\displaystyle\tilde{D}^{K}_{Z}\;=\;\tilde{f}(\mathbf{Z}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{Z}_{n})\;.

Denoting
g=h\circ\tilde{f}
, we can then write the difference in expectation in terms of Lindeberg’s telescoping sum as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[h(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K})-h(\tilde{D}^{K}_{Z})]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[g(\mathbf{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{V}_{1})-g(\mathbf{Z}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{Z}_{n})]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\mathbb{E}[g(\mathbf{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{V}_{i-1},\mathbf{V}_{i},\mathbf{Z}_{i+1},\ldots,\mathbf{Z}_{n})

\displaystyle\;\qquad\qquad-g(\mathbf{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{V}_{i-1},\;\mathbf{Z}_{i},\;\mathbf{Z}_{i+1},\ldots,\mathbf{Z}_{n})]\big)\;.

Since each summand differs only in the
i
-th argument, we can perform a second-order Taylor expansion about the
i
-th argument provided that the function
h
such that
h
is twice-differentiable. The second-order remainder term is further “Taylor-expanded” to an additional
\epsilon
-order for any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
by choosing
h^{\prime\prime}
to be
\epsilon
-Hölder. Write
D_{i}
as the differential operator with respect to the
i
-th argument and denote
\tilde{f}_{i}(\mathbf{x})\coloneqq\tilde{f}(\mathbf{V}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{V}_{i-1},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{Z}_{i+1},\ldots,\mathbf{Z}_{n})
. Then informally speaking, the Taylor expansion argument amounts to bounding each summand as

\displaystyle\big|\text{(summand)}_{i}\big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[D_{i}(h\circ\tilde{f}_{i})(0)(\mathbf{V}_{i}-\mathbf{Z}_{i})]+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\mathbb{E}[D_{i}^{2}(h\circ\tilde{f}_{i})(0)(\mathbf{V}_{i}^{2}-\mathbf{Z}_{i}^{2})]

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{6}$}}\big(\text{H\"{o}lder constant of }h^{\prime\prime}\big)\times\mathbb{E}\big[\big|D_{i}\tilde{f}_{i}(0)\mathbf{V}_{i}\big|^{2+\epsilon}+\big|D_{i}\tilde{f}_{i}(0)\mathbf{Z}_{i}\big|^{2+\epsilon}\big]\;,

where we have used the fact that
\tilde{f}_{i}
is a linear function in expressing the last quantity. The first two terms vanish because
h\circ\tilde{f}_{i}
is independent of
\big(\mathbf{V}_{i},\mathbf{Z}_{i}\big)
and the first two moments of
\mathbf{V}_{i}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{i}
match. The third term is bounded carefully by noting the moment structure of
\mathbf{V}_{i}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{i}
to give the error term
\frac{1}{n}E_{\delta;K}
. Summing the errors over
1\leq i\leq n
then gives the Gaussian approximation error bound in Lemma
39
.

Proof of Lemma
39

For any
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
and
\delta>0
, let
h_{\tau;\delta}
be the twice-differentiable function defined in Lemma
34
(i.e.
m=2
), which satisfies

h_{\tau+\delta;\delta}(x)\;\leq\;\mathbb{I}_{\{x>\tau\}}\;\leq\;h_{\tau;\delta}(x)\;.

By applying the above bounds with
\tau
set to
t-\delta
and
t-2\delta
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t-\delta)-\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t-2\delta)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{I}_{\{\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t-\delta\}}-\mathbb{I}_{\{\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t-2\delta\}}]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[h_{t-\delta;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K})-h_{t-\delta;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K})]\;,

and similarly

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t+2\delta)-\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t+\delta)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{I}_{\{\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t+2\delta\}}-\mathbb{I}_{\{\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t+\delta\}}]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[h_{t+2\delta;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K})-h_{t+2\delta;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K})]\;.

Therefore, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t-\delta)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t-2\delta)+E^{\prime}_{\delta;K}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t+\delta)\;\geq\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t+2\delta)-E^{\prime}_{\delta;K}\;,

(14)

where
E^{\prime}_{\delta;K}\coloneqq\sup\nolimits_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}}|\mathbb{E}[h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K})-h_{\tau;\delta}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K})]|
. The next step is to bound
E^{\prime}_{\delta;K}
, to which we apply Lindeberg’s technique for proving central limit theorem. We denote the scaled mean as

\displaystyle\tilde{\mu}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean[\bV_1]}{\sigma^{1/2} (n(n-1))^{1/4}}$}}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean[\bZ_1]}{\sigma^{1/2} (n(n-1))^{1/4}}$}}\;,

and define the centred and scaled versions of
\mathbf{V}_{i}
and
\mathbf{Z}_{i}
respectively as

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\bV_i}{\sigma^{1/2} (n(n-1))^{1/4}}$}}-\tilde{\mu}\;,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\bZ_i}{\sigma^{1/2} (n(n-1))^{1/4}}$}}-\tilde{\mu}\;.

We also define the function
f:(\mathbb{R}^{K})^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

\displaystyle f(\mathbf{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{v}_{n})\coloneqq\msum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}(\mathbf{v}_{i}+\tilde{\mu})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}(\mathbf{v}_{j}+\tilde{\mu})\;,\;\;\text{ where we recall }\Lambda^{K}\coloneqq\text{\rm diag}\{\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{K}\}\;.

This allows us to express the random quantities in (
14
) as

\displaystyle\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}\;=\;f(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{n})\;,

\displaystyle\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}\;=\;f(\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})\;.

By defining the random function

\displaystyle F_{i}(\mathbf{v})\;:=\;f(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i-1},\mathbf{v},\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i+1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})

\displaystyle\text{ for }\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}\text{ and }1\leq i\leq n\;,

we can write
E^{\prime}_{\delta;K}
into Lindeberg’s telescoping sum as

\displaystyle E^{\prime}_{\delta;K}\;=

\displaystyle\;\sup\nolimits_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}}|\mathbb{E}[h_{\tau;\delta}\circ f(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{n})-h_{\tau;\delta}\circ f(\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})]|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\sup\nolimits_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\msum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[h_{\tau;\delta}(F_{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i})-h_{\tau;\delta}(F_{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}))]\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\sup\nolimits_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}|\mathbb{E}[h_{\tau;\delta}\circ F_{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i})-h_{\tau;\delta}\circ F_{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i})]|\;.

Since
h_{\tau;\delta}\circ f
is twice-differentiable, by a second-order Taylor expansion around
\mathbf{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
, there exists random values
\theta_{V},\theta_{Z}\in(0,1)
almost surely such that

\displaystyle h_{\tau;\delta}\circ F_{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i})\;

\displaystyle=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{0}}\,\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\theta_{V}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}}\,\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}^{\otimes 2}\;,

\displaystyle h_{\tau;\delta}\circ F_{i}(\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i})\;

\displaystyle=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{0}}\,\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\theta_{Z}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}}\,\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}^{\otimes 2}\;.

Substituting this into the sum above gives

\displaystyle E^{\prime}_{\delta;K}\;\leq\;\sup\nolimits_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}}\Big(

\displaystyle\msum_{i=1}^{n}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{0}}\,\big(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}-\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i})\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\theta_{V}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}}\,\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}^{\otimes 2}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\theta_{Z}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}}\,\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}^{\otimes 2}\Big]\Big|\,\Big)\;.

The first sum vanishes because the only randomness of the derivative comes from
F_{i}
, who is independent of
(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i},\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i})
, and the mean of
\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}
match. To handle the second sum, we make use of independence again and the fact that the second moment of
\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}
also match: By subtracting and adding the term

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{0}}(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i})^{\otimes 2}\Big]\;=\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{0}}(\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i})^{\otimes 2}\Big]\;,

we can apply a triangle inequality to get that

\displaystyle E^{\prime}_{\delta;K}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\sup\nolimits_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}}\Big(

\displaystyle\msum_{i=1}^{n}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\theta_{V}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{0}}\Big)\,\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}^{\otimes 2}\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\;+\msum_{i=1}^{n}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\theta_{Z}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{0}}\Big)\,\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}^{\otimes 2}\Big]\Big|\,\Big)\;.

(15)

The final step is to bound the two sums by exploiting the derivative structure of
h_{\tau;\delta}
and
F_{i}
. Note that
F_{i}
is a linear function: its first derivative is given by

\displaystyle\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{x})\;=\;2\msum_{1\leq j<i}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{j}+2\msum_{i<j\leq n}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{j}+2(n-1)\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mu}\;\in\;\mathbb{R}^{K}\;,

which is independent of
\mathbf{x}
, while its higher derivatives vanish. By a second-order chain rule, this implies that almost surely

\displaystyle\;\Big|\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\theta_{V}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{0}}\Big)\,\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}^{\otimes 2}\Big|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big|\big(\partial^{2}h_{\tau;\delta}\big(F_{i}(\theta_{V}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i})\big)-\partial^{2}h_{\tau;\delta}\big(F_{i}(\mathbf{0})\big)\big)\,(\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i})^{2}\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\big|\partial^{2}h_{\tau;\delta}\big(F_{i}(\theta_{V}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i})\big)-\partial^{2}h_{\tau;\delta}\big(F_{i}(\mathbf{0})\big)\big|\,\big|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}\big|^{2}\;.

For
\nu\in(2,3]
, by the Hölder property of
\partial^{2}h_{\tau;\delta}
from Lemma
34
, we get that almost surely,

\displaystyle\big|\partial^{2}h_{\tau;\delta}(F_{i}(\theta_{V}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}))-\partial^{2}h_{\tau;\delta}(F_{i}(\mathbf{0}))\big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;18\times 3^{\nu-2}\delta^{-\nu}|F_{i}(\theta_{V}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i})-F_{i}(\mathbf{0})|^{\nu-2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;18\times 3^{\nu-2}\delta^{-\nu}|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}(\theta_{V}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i})|^{\nu-2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;54\delta^{-\nu}|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}|^{\nu-2}\;.

In the last inequality, we have used that
\theta_{V}
takes value in
[0,1]
. Combining the results, we get that each summand in the first sum in (
15
) can be bounded as

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\theta_{V}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{0}}\Big)\,\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}^{\otimes 2}\Big]\Big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;54\delta^{-\nu}\mathbb{E}\big[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}|^{\nu}\big]\;.

The exact same argument applies to the summands of the second sum to give

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\theta_{Z}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^2 h_{\tau;\delta} \circ F_i(\bx)}{\partial\bx^2}$}}\Big|_{\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{0}}\Big)\,\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}^{\otimes 2}\Big]\Big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;54\delta^{-\nu}\mathbb{E}\big[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}|^{\nu}\big]\;,

so a substitution back into (
15
) gives

\displaystyle E^{\prime}_{\delta;K}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;27\delta^{-\nu}\msum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}|^{\nu}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}|^{\nu}\big]\Big)\;.

We defer to Lemma
42
to show that there exists an absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that the moment terms can be bounded as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}|^{\nu}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}|^{\nu}\big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C'}{n^{\nu/2}}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mfullnu)^\nu+
\varepsilon_{K;\nu}^\nu}{\sigma^{\nu} }$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mcondnu)^\nu+ \varepsilon_{K;\nu}^{\nu} }{(n-1)^{-\nu/2}\,\sigma^{\nu}}$}}\Big)\;.

(16)

Combining with (
14
) and defining
E_{\delta;K}
to be the upper bound for
E^{\prime}_{\delta;K}
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t-\delta)\;\leq\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t-2\delta)+E_{\delta;K}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{n}^{K}>t+\delta)\;\geq\;\mathbb{P}(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}>t+2\delta)-E_{\delta;K}\;,

where we have made the
K
-dependence explicit and define, for
C\coloneqq 27C^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle E_{\delta;K}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C}{\delta^\nu n^{\nu/2-1}}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mfullnu)^\nu+
\varepsilon_{K;\nu}^\nu}{\sigma^{\nu} }$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mcondnu)^\nu+ \varepsilon_{K;\nu}^{\nu} }{(n-1)^{-\nu/2}\,\sigma^{\nu}}$}}\Big)\;.

\jmlrQED

Lemma 42
.

(
16
) holds.

Proof of Lemma
42

We seek to bound
\mathbb{E}[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}|^{\nu}]+\mathbb{E}[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}|^{\nu}]
for
\nu\in(2,3]
and

\displaystyle\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})\;=\;2\msum_{1\leq j<i}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{j}+2\msum_{i<j\leq n}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{j}+2(n-1)\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mu}\;\in\;\mathbb{R}^{K}\;.

We first focus on bounding the first expectation. By convexity of the function
x\mapsto|x|^{\nu}
, we can apply Jensen’s inequality to bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}|^{\nu}\big]\;=\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|2\msum_{j<i}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{j}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}+2\msum_{j>i}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{j}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}+2(n-1)\tilde{\mu}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}\Big|^{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{3}$}}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|6\msum_{j<i}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{j}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}\big|^{\nu}\big]+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{3}$}}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|6\msum_{j>i}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{j}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}\big|^{\nu}\big]+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{3}$}}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|6(n-1)\tilde{\mu}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big|^{\nu}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;72\big(\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\msum_{j<i}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{j}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}\big|^{\nu}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\msum_{j>i}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{j}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}\big|^{\nu}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[\big|(n-1)\tilde{\mu}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big|^{\nu}\big]\big)\;,

where we have noted that
\nu\leq 3
. Since
\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}
’s are i.i.d.,
\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}
’s are i.i.d. and all variables involved are zero-mean,
(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{j}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i})_{j=1}^{i-1}
forms a martingale difference sequence with respect to the filtration
\sigma(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i},\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}),\ldots,\sigma(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i},\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i-1})
, and so is
(\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{j}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i})_{j=i+1}^{n}
with respect to the filtration
\sigma(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i},\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i+1}),\ldots,\sigma(\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i},\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i+1},\ldots,\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{n})
. This allows the above two moments of sums to be bounded via the martigale moment inequality from Lemma
26
: There exists an absolute constant
C_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}|^{\nu}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C_{0}\Big((i-1)^{\nu/2-1}\msum_{j=1}^{i-1}\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{j}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}|^{\nu}]+(n-i)^{\nu/2-1}\msum_{j=i+1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{j}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}|^{\nu}]

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\;\;+(n-1)^{\nu}\,\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\mu}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}|^{\nu}]\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C_{0}(n-1)^{\nu/2}\Big(\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2}|^{\nu}]+\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}|^{\nu}]+(n-1)^{\nu/2}\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\mu}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}|^{\nu}]\Big)\;.

By the exact same argument, the other expectation we want to bound can also be controlled as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}|^{\nu}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\;C_{0}(n-1)^{\nu/2}\Big(\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{2}|^{\nu}]+\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}|^{\nu}]+(n-1)^{\nu/2}\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\mu}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1}|^{\nu}]\Big)\;.

Finally, we relate these moments terms to moments of
u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})
, up to error terms that vanish as
K\rightarrow\infty
: Denoting
\mu_{k}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})]
, we have that by Lemma
31
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\mu}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}|^{\nu}]\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sigma^{\nu} n^{\nu/2}(n-1)^{\nu/2}}$}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}(\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})-\mu_{k})\mu_{k}\Big|^{\nu}\Big]\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
4 ( (\Mcondnu)^\nu+ \varepsilon_{K;\nu}^{\nu} )}{\sigma^{\nu}\, n^{\nu/2}(n-1)^{\nu/2}}$}}\;,

and for some absolute constant
C_{1}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{2}|^{\nu}]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sigma^{\nu} n^{\nu/2}(n-1)^{\nu/2}}$}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}(\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})-\mu_{k})(\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})-\mu_{k})\Big|^{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 C_1 (\Mfullnu)^\nu-
\frac{1}{2} (\Mcondnu)^\nu+
(4 C_1 + 2) \varepsilon_{K;\nu}^\nu}{\sigma^{\nu} n^{\nu/2}(n-1)^{\nu/2}}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 C_1 (\Mfullnu)^\nu+
(4 C_1 + 2) \varepsilon_{K;\nu}^\nu}{\sigma^{\nu} n^{\nu/2}(n-1)^{\nu/2}}$}}\;.

For the moment terms involving the Gaussians
\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{2}
, we apply Lemma
32
to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\mu}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1}|^{\nu}]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean[ | (\mean[\bV_1])^\top\Lambda^K \bZ_1 |^\nu]}{\sigma^{\nu} n^{\nu/2}(n-1)^{\nu/2}}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
7 (\sigcond^\nu+ 8 \varepsilon_{K;2}^\nu)
}{\sigma^{\nu} n^{\nu/2}(n-1)^{\nu/2}}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
7 ((\Mcondnu)^\nu+ 8 \varepsilon_{K;\nu}^\nu)
}{\sigma^{\nu} n^{\nu/2}(n-1)^{\nu/2}}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{2}|^{\nu}]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean[ | \bZ_1^\top\Lambda^K \bZ_2 |^\nu]}{\sigma^{\nu} n^{\nu/2}(n-1)^{\nu/2}}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
6 ( \sigfull^\nu+ \varepsilon_{K;2}^\nu)
}{\sigma^{\nu} n^{\nu/2}(n-1)^{\nu/2}}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
6 ( (\Mfullnu)^\nu+ \varepsilon_{K;\nu}^\nu)
}{\sigma^{\nu} n^{\nu/2}(n-1)^{\nu/2}}$}}\;.

In the last inequalities for both bounds, we have noted that
L_{2}
norm is dominated by
L_{\nu}
norm since
\nu>2
. Meanwhile by Lemma
32
again, there exists some absolute constant
C_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{1}^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{1}|^{\nu}]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean[ | (\bV_1 - \mean[\bV_1] )^\top\Lambda^K \bZ_1 |^\nu]}{\sigma^{\nu} n^{\nu/2}(n-1)^{\nu/2}}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{8 C_2 (\Mfullnu)^\nu+ (8C_2+4) \varepsilon_{K;\nu}^\nu}{\sigma^{\nu} n^{\nu/2}(n-1)^{\nu/2}}$}}\;.

Substituting the five moment bounds into the earlier bounds on
\mathbb{E}[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}|^{\nu}]
and
\mathbb{E}[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}|^{\nu}]
and combining the constant terms, we get that there exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{i}|^{\nu}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[|\partial F_{i}(\mathbf{0})^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{Z}}_{i}|^{\nu}\big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C}{n^{\nu/2}}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mfullnu)^\nu+
\varepsilon_{K;\nu}^\nu}{\sigma^{\nu} }$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mcondnu)^\nu+ \varepsilon_{K;\nu}^{\nu} }{(n-1)^{-\nu/2}\,\sigma^{\nu}}$}}\Big)\;.

\jmlrQED

C.5
Proof of Lemma
40

Proof overview.
For convenience, we write

\displaystyle U_{0}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)}$}}\msum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}(\eta^{K}_{i})^{\top}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{j}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{i}\;,

so that

\displaystyle\tilde{D}^{K}_{Z}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}U_{0}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\mu^{K}\;,

\displaystyle\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}U_{0}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}D\;.

To approximate the distribution of
\tilde{D}^{K}_{Z}
by that of
\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}
, the proof boils down to replacing
(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\mu^{K}
by
D
. We use a Markov-type argument so that we obtain an error term that is separate from the distribution terms.

Proof of Lemma
40

Recall that Lemma
35
allows us to approximate the distribution of a sum of two random variables by a single one provided that the other is negligible. Writing

\displaystyle\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}\;=\;\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}+(\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}-\tilde{U}_{n}^{K})\;=\;\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}\big((\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\mu^{K}-D\big)\;,

we can apply Lemma
35
to obtain that for any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}\leq b)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\big(a-\epsilon\leq\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\leq b+\epsilon\big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}\big|(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\mu^{K}-D\big|\geq\epsilon\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq\tilde{D}_{Z}^{K}\leq b)\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}(a+\epsilon\leq\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\leq b-\epsilon)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}\big|(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\mu^{K}-D\big|\geq\epsilon\Big)\;.

Note that
|(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\mu^{K}-D|
is deterministic. By a Markov inequality and the bound from Lemma
31
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}\big|(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\mu^{K}-D\big|\geq\epsilon\Big)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\epsilon\sigma}$}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\mu^{K}-D\big|\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \big| \msum_{k=1}^K \lambda_K \mu_k^2 - D \big|}{ \epsilon\, n^{-1/2} (n-1)^{-1/2} \sigma}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \varepsilon_{K;1}}{ \epsilon\, n^{-1/2} (n-1)^{-1/2} \sigma}$}}\;.

Combining the two results gives the desired bounds.

\jmlrQED

C.6
Proof of Lemma
41

Proof overview.
The key ingredient of the proof is Theorem 8 of
Carbery and Wright 2001
, which gives an anti-concentration bound for the distribution of a polynomial of Gaussians in terms of its variance. In Lemma
36
, we have rewritten the result in the special case of a degree-two polynomial, which allows us to control the distribution of
\tilde{U}^{K}_{n}
in terms of its variance.

We introduce some matrix shorthands: For any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, denote
O_{m}
as the zero matrix in
\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
,
J_{m}
as the all-one matrix in
\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
and
I_{m}
as the identity matrix in
\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
. Define the
nK\times nK
matrix
M
as

\displaystyle M\;\coloneqq\;\begin{pmatrix}O_{K}&\Lambda^{K}&\ldots&\Lambda^{K}\\
\Lambda^{K}&O_{K}&\ddots&\vdots\\
\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&\Lambda^{K}\\
\Lambda^{K}&\ldots&\Lambda^{K}&O_{K}\\
\end{pmatrix}\;=\;\Lambda^{K}\otimes(J_{n}-I_{n})\;,

as well as

\displaystyle\mu\coloneqq\big((\mu^{K})^{\top},\ldots,(\mu^{K})^{\top}\big)^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{nK}\;,\;\;\Sigma\coloneqq\Sigma^{K}\otimes I_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{nK\times nK}\;,\;\;\Lambda\coloneqq\Lambda^{K}\otimes I_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{nK\times nK}\;.

We also consider the concatenated
nK
-dimensional standard Gaussian vector

\displaystyle\eta\;\coloneqq\;\big((\eta_{1}^{K})^{\top},\ldots,(\eta_{n}^{K})^{\top}\big)^{\top}\;.

Proof of Lemma
41

The goal is to bound the distribution function between
a\leq b\in\mathbb{R}
of

\displaystyle\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}\,U_{n}^{K}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n(n-1)}}$}}\msum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}(\eta^{K}_{i})^{\top}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{j}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \sqrt{n-1}}{\sigma\sqrt{n} }$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{i}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}\,D

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n(n-1)}}$}}\eta^{\top}\Sigma^{1/2}M\Sigma^{1/2}\eta+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \sqrt{n-1}}{\sigma\sqrt{n} }$}}\mu^{\top}\Lambda\Sigma^{1/2}\eta+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}\,D\;.

For convenience, define

\displaystyle Q_{1}\;\coloneqq\;\eta^{\top}\Sigma^{1/2}M\Sigma^{1/2}\eta\;,\qquad Q_{2}\;\coloneqq\;\mu^{\top}\Lambda\Sigma^{1/2}\eta\;,\qquad\tilde{U}_{0}\;\coloneqq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sigma\sqrt{n(n-1)}}$}}Q_{1}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \sqrt{n-1}}{\sigma\sqrt{n} }$}}Q_{2}\;.

Denote
\alpha\coloneqq\frac{b-a}{2}
and
\beta\coloneqq\frac{a+b}{2}
. Rewriting the probability in terms of
\tilde{U}_{0}
,
\alpha
and
\beta
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\leq b)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\Big((\beta-\alpha)\,\leq\tilde{U}_{0}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}\,D\leq\,(\beta+\alpha)\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big|\tilde{U}_{0}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}$}}\,D-\beta\Big|\;\leq\;\alpha\Big)\;.

Since
\tilde{U}_{0}+\frac{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma}\,D-\beta
is a degree-two polynomial of
\eta
, we can apply Lemma
36
to bound the above probability: For an absolute constant
C^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\leq b)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}\alpha^{1/2}\big(\text{\rm Var}[\tilde{U}_{0}]\big)^{-1/4}\;,

(17)

where the variance term can be expanded as

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}\big[\tilde{U}_{0}\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)\sigma^2}$}}\text{\rm Var}[Q_{1}]+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 (n-1)}{n \sigma^2}$}}\text{\rm Var}[Q_{2}]+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4}{n \, \sigma^2}$}}\,\text{\rm Cov}[Q_{1},Q_{2}]\;.

We now provide bound the individual terms in the variance. By noting that each summand in
Q_{1}
is zero-mean when
i\neq j
and that each summand in
Q_{2}
is zero-mean, the covariance term can be computed as

\displaystyle\text{\rm Cov}[Q_{1},Q_{2}]\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}\msum_{l=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\Big[(\eta^{K}_{i})^{\top}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{j}\times(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{l}\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\mathbb{E}\Big[(\eta^{K}_{1})^{\top}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{1}\times(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{1}\Big]\;.

Denote
\xi_{k}
as the
k
-th coordinate of
\eta_{1}^{K}
. Then the above expectation is taken over a linear combination of terms of the form
\xi_{k_{1}}\xi_{k_{2}}\xi_{k_{3}}
. If any of
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}
is distinct from the other two indices, the expectation is zero; if
k_{1}=k_{2}=k_{3}
, the expectation is again zero by property of a standard Gauassian. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\text{\rm Cov}[Q_{1},Q_{2}]\;=\;0\;.

On the other hand, the first variance can be computed by using the moment formula for a quadratic form of Gaussian from Lemma
27
and the cyclic property of trace:

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[Q_{1}]\;=

\displaystyle\;2\text{\rm Tr}\big((\Sigma^{1/2}M\Sigma^{1/2})^{2}\big)\;=\;2\text{\rm Tr}\big((\Sigma M)^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;2\text{\rm Tr}\big((\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K})^{2}\otimes(J_{n}-I_{n})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;2\text{\rm Tr}\big((\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K})^{2}\otimes J_{n}^{2}\big)-4\text{\rm Tr}\big((\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K})^{2}\otimes J_{n}\big)+2\text{\rm Tr}\big((\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K})^{2}\otimes I_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\big(2n^{2}-4n+2n\big)\text{\rm Tr}\big((\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;2n(n-1)\text{\rm Tr}\big((\Lambda^{K}\Sigma^{K})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;2n(n-1)(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}\;.

In the last inequality, we have used the bound from Lemma
32
on
\text{\rm Tr}\big((\Lambda^{K}\Sigma^{K})^{2}\big)
. The second variance is on a Gaussian random variable and can be bounded by Lemma
32
again as

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[Q_{2}]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mu^{\top}\Lambda\Sigma\Lambda\mu\;=\;n(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K}\mu^{K}\;\geq\;n(\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}-2\sigma_{\rm cond}\varepsilon_{K;2}-4\varepsilon_{K;2})\;.

This implies that

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}\big[\tilde{U}_{0}\big]\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{\sigma^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 (n-1)}{\sigma^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}-2\sigma_{\rm cond}\varepsilon_{K;2}-4\varepsilon_{K;2})\;.

Substituting this into (
17
) and redefining the constants, we get that there exists an absolute constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\leq b)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C(b-a)^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sigma^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n-1}{\sigma^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}-2\sigma_{\rm cond}\varepsilon_{K;2}-4\varepsilon_{K;2})\Big)^{-1/4}\;.

\jmlrQED

Appendix D
Proofs for the remaining results in
Section

3

D.1
Proofs for variants and corollaries of the main result

The upper bound in Proposition
6
is a concentration inequality and is obtained by a standard argument via Chebyshev’s inequality. The lower bound is a combination of the anti-concentration bound for a Gaussian quadratic form from Lemma
41
and
Theorem

2
.

Proof of Proposition
6

Denote

\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\coloneqq\frac{\sqrt{n(n-1)}U_{n}^{K}}{\sigma_{\rm max}}
. In Lemma
41
, we have shown that for any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
with
a\leq b
, there exists some absolute constant
C^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq\tilde{U}_{n}^{K}\leq b)\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}(b-a)^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sigmax^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n-1}{\sigmax^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}-2\sigma_{\rm cond}\varepsilon_{K;2}-4\varepsilon_{K;2})\Big)^{-1/4}\;.

Take
K\rightarrow\infty
and using
2
for
\nu\geq 2
, we get that
\varepsilon_{K;2}\rightarrow 0
. For a fixed
\epsilon>0
, set
a=\frac{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma_{\rm max}}D-\epsilon
and
b=\frac{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma_{\rm max}}D+\epsilon
, we get that

\displaystyle\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigmax}$}}|U_{n}^{K}-D|\leq\epsilon\Big)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\sqrt{2}\,C^{\prime}\,\epsilon^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigfull^2}{\sigmax^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(n-1)\sigcond^2}{\sigmax^2}$}}\Big)^{-1/4}\;\leq\;\sqrt{2}\,C^{\prime}\,\epsilon^{1/2}\;.

Now by
Theorem

2
, there exists an absolute constant
C^{\prime\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigmax}$}}D_{n}>t\Big)-\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigmax}$}}U_{n}^{K}>t\Big)\Big|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime\prime}\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{4\nu+2}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\Mmaxnu}{\sigmax}$}}\Big)^{\frac{\nu}{2\nu+1}}\;.

By a triangle inequality, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigmax}$}}|D_{n}-D|>\epsilon\Big)\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigmax}$}}|U_{n}^{K}-D|>\epsilon\Big)-2C^{\prime\prime}\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{4\nu+2}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\Mmaxnu}{\sigmax}$}}\Big)^{\frac{\nu}{2\nu+1}}

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;1-\sqrt{2}\,C^{\prime}\epsilon^{1/2}-2C^{\prime\prime}\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{4\nu+2}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\Mmaxnu}{\sigmax}$}}\Big)^{\frac{\nu}{2\nu+1}}\;.

By replacing
\epsilon
with
\frac{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigma_{\rm max}}\epsilon
and redefining constants, we get the desired lower bound that there exists absolute constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|D_{n}-D|>\epsilon)\;\geq

\displaystyle\;1-C_{1}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigmax}$}}\Big)^{1/2}\epsilon^{1/2}-C_{2}\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{4\nu+2}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\Mmaxnu}{\sigmax}$}}\Big)^{\frac{\nu}{2\nu+1}}\;.

For the upper bound, we apply a Chebyshev inequality directly to
D_{n}
and bound the variance by Lemma
30
: There exists some absolute constant
C^{\prime}_{3}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|D_{n}-D|>\epsilon)\;\leq\;\epsilon^{-2}\text{\rm Var}[D_{n}]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}_{3}\epsilon^{-2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigcond^2}{n^{-1}(n-1)^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigfull^2}{(n-1)^2}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}_{3}\epsilon^{-2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigmax}{n-1}$}}\Big)^{2}\;\leq\;C_{3}\epsilon^{-2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigmax}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}$}}\Big)^{2}\;.

In the last inequality, we have noted that
\frac{1}{n-1}\leq\frac{2}{n}
for
n\geq 2
and defined
C_{3}=2C^{\prime}_{3}
. This finishes the proof.

\jmlrQED

Theorem

2
provides an approximation of the distribution of
D_{n}
by that of a Gaussian quadratic form. Proposition
9
combines
Theorem

2
with a Markov argument, which makes a further approximation of the Gaussian quadratic form by a weighted sum of chi-squares
U_{n}^{K}
. The approximation error introduced vanishes as
n,d
grow provided that
\rho_{d}=\omega(n^{1/2})
, i.e.
n^{-1/2}\sigma_{\rm full}=\omega(\sigma_{\rm cond})
.

Proof of Proposition
9

We first seek to compare
W_{n}^{K}
to the distribution of

\displaystyle U_{n}^{K}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)}$}}\msum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}(\eta^{K}_{i})^{\top}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{j}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{i}+D\;,

where
\{\eta^{K}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are i.i.d. standard Gaussian vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{K}
. The first step is to write

\displaystyle U_{n}^{K}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n-1}}{\sqrt{n}}$}}W_{0}+D+\Big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n-1}}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\Big)W_{0}+W_{1}+W_{2}\;,

where we have defined the zero-mean random variables

\displaystyle W_{0}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)}$}}\Big(\msum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\eta^{K}_{i})^{\top}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{j}-n\text{\rm Tr}(\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K})\Big)\;,

\displaystyle W_{1}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)}$}}\Big(\msum_{i=1}^{n}(\eta^{K}_{i})^{\top}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{i}-n\text{\rm Tr}(\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K})\Big)\;,

\displaystyle W_{2}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{i}\;.

Fix
\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}>0
. We first use the bound from Lemma
35
: For any
