a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(a\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n-1}}{\sqrt{n}}$}}W_{0}+D\Big)\leq b\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\Big(a-\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2}\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}U_{n}^{K}\leq b+\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\;+\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}\Big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n-1}}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\Big)|W_{0}|\geq\epsilon_{0}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}|W_{1}|\geq\epsilon_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\;+\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}|W_{2}|\geq\epsilon_{2}\Big)\;

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(a\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n-1}}{\sqrt{n}}$}}W_{0}+D\Big)\leq b\Big)

\displaystyle\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\Big(a+\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}U_{n}^{K}\leq b-\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\;-\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}\Big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n-1}}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\Big)|W_{0}|\geq\epsilon_{0}\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}|W_{1}|\geq\epsilon_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\;-\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}|W_{2}|\geq\epsilon_{2}\Big)\;.

We now bound the error terms. By the Chebyshev’s inequality, the variance formula of a quadratic form of Gaussians from Lemma
27
and the bound from Lemma
32
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}|W_{1}|\geq\epsilon_{1}\Big)\;\leq\;\epsilon_{1}^{-2}\text{\rm Var}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}W_{1}\Big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{\epsilon_1^2 (n-1) \sigfull^2}$}}\text{\rm Tr}\big((\Lambda^{K}\Sigma^{K})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 (\sigfull+\varepsilon_{K;2})^2}{\epsilon_1^2 (n-1) \sigfull^2}$}}\;.

Similarly, by the Chebyshev’s inequality, the variance formula of a Gaussian and the bound from Lemma
32
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}|W_{2}|\geq\epsilon\Big)\;\leq\;\epsilon_{2}^{-2}\text{\rm Var}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}W_{2}\Big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4(n-1)}{\epsilon_2^2 \sigfull^2}$}}\mathbb{E}\big[(\mu^{K})^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K}\mu^{K}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4(n-1)(\sigcond+2\varepsilon_{K;2})^2}{\epsilon_2^2 \sigfull^2}$}}\;.

By Lemma
33
, we can replace
W_{0}
by using the following equality in distribution:

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n-1}}{\sqrt{n}}$}}W_{0}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^{3/2}(n-1)^{1/2}}$}}\Big(\msum_{i,j=1}^{n}(\eta^{K}_{i})^{\top}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{j}-n\text{\rm Tr}(\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K})\Big)

\displaystyle\;\overset{d}{=}

\displaystyle\;W_{n}^{K}-D\;.

Finally, using a Chebyshev’s inequality together with the moment bound in Lemma
33
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}\Big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n-1}}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\Big)|W_{0}|\geq\epsilon_{0}\Big)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n(n-1)}{ \epsilon_0^2 \sigfull^2}$}}\Big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n-1}}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\Big)^{2}\text{\rm Var}\big[W_{0}\big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n^2}{ \epsilon_0^2 \sigfull^2}$}}\Big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n-1}}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\Big)^{2}\text{\rm Var}\big[W_{n}^{K}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2n (\sigfull+\varepsilon_{K;2})^2}{\epsilon_0^2 (n-1) \sigfull^2}$}}\Big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n-1}}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 (\sigfull+\varepsilon_{K;2})^2}{\epsilon_0^2 (n-1) \sigfull^2}$}}\;.

In the last inequality, we have noted that
\sqrt{n}-\sqrt{n-1}\leq 1
. Combining the above bounds, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(a\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}W_{n}^{K}\leq b\Big)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\Big(a-\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2}\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}U_{n}^{K}\leq b+\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 (\sigfull+\varepsilon_{K;2})^2}{(n-1) \sigfull^2}$}}\big(\epsilon_{0}^{-2}+\epsilon_{1}^{-2}\big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4(n-1)(\sigcond+2\varepsilon_{K;2})^2}{\epsilon_2^2 \sigfull^2}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(a\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}W_{n}^{K}\leq b\Big)\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}\Big(a+\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}U_{n}^{K}\leq b-\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 (\sigfull+\varepsilon_{K;2})^2}{(n-1) \sigfull^2}$}}\big(\epsilon_{0}^{-2}+\epsilon_{1}^{-2}\big)-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4(n-1)(\sigcond+2\varepsilon_{K;2})^2}{\epsilon_2^2 \sigfull^2}$}}\;.

Taking
b\rightarrow\infty
and
a\rightarrow t
from the right, we get that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}W_{n}^{K}>t\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}U_{n}^{K}>t\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\max\Big\{\mathbb{P}\Big(t-\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2}\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}U_{n}^{K}\leq t\Big)\;,\;\mathbb{P}\Big(t\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}U_{n}^{K}\leq\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 (\sigfull+\varepsilon_{K;2})^2}{(n-1) \sigfull^2}$}}\big(\epsilon_{0}^{-2}+\epsilon_{1}^{-2}\big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4(n-1)(\sigcond+2\varepsilon_{K;2})^2}{\epsilon_2^2 \sigfull^2}$}}\;.

This allows us to follow a similar argument to the proof of
Theorem

2
to approximate
W_{n}^{K}
by
U_{n}^{K}
. To bound the maxima, we apply Lemma
41
with
\sigma=\sigma_{\rm full}
: There exists some absolute constant
C^{\prime}
such that for any
a\leq b\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(a\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}

\displaystyle U_{n}^{K}\leq b\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}(b-a)^{1/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sigfull^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n-1}{\sigfull^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}-2\sigma_{\rm cond}\varepsilon_{K;2}-4\varepsilon_{K;2})\Big)^{-1/4}\;.

By additionally noting that
(\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2})^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}+\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}}+\sqrt{\epsilon_{2}}
, we get that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}W_{n}^{K}>t\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}U_{n}^{K}>t\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}(\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}+\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}}+\sqrt{\epsilon_{2}})\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sigfull^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n-1}{\sigfull^2}$}}(\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}-2\sigma_{\rm cond}\varepsilon_{K;2}-4\varepsilon_{K;2})\Big)^{-1/4}

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 (\sigfull+\varepsilon_{K;2})^2}{(n-1) \sigfull^2}$}}\big(\epsilon_{0}^{-2}+\epsilon_{1}^{-2}\big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4(n-1)(\sigcond+2\varepsilon_{K;2})^2}{\epsilon_2^2 \sigfull^2}$}}\;.

Taking
K\rightarrow\infty
on both sides, the inequality becomes

\displaystyle\Big|\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}W_{n}^{K}>t\Big)-\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}U_{n}^{K}>t\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}(\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}+\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}}+\sqrt{\epsilon_{2}})\Big(1+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(n-1)\sigcond^2}{\sigfull^2}$}}\Big)^{-1/4}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n-1}$}}\big(\epsilon_{0}^{-2}+\epsilon_{1}^{-2}\big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4(n-1) \sigcond^2}{\epsilon_2^2 \sigfull^2}$}}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}(\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}}+\sqrt{\epsilon_{1}}+\sqrt{\epsilon_{2}})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n-1}$}}\big(\epsilon_{0}^{-2}+\epsilon_{1}^{-2}\big)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4(n-1) \sigcond^2}{\epsilon_2^2 \sigfull^2}$}}\;.

Choosing
\epsilon_{0}=\epsilon_{1}=(n-1)^{-2/5}
and
\epsilon_{2}=\big((n-1)\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}/\sigma_{\rm full}^{2}\big)^{2/5}
, redefining constants and taking a supremum over
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we get that there exists some absolute constant
C^{\prime\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}W_{n}^{K}>t\Big)-\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}U_{n}^{K}>t\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime\prime}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(n-1)^{1/5}}$}}+\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n-1}\, \sigcond}{\sigfull}$}}\Big)^{2/5}\Big)\;.

The final step is to relate this bound to
D_{n}
. Consider the last step (
13
) of the proof of
Theorem

2
in
Section

C.2
. If we set
\sigma=\sigma_{\rm full}
instead of
\sigma_{\rm max}
, we get that there exists some absolute constant
C^{\prime\prime\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}D_{n}>t\Big)-\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}U_{n}^{K}>t\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\;C^{\prime\prime\prime}\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{4\nu+2}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mfullnu)^\nu}{\sigfull^{\nu} }$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mcondnu)^\nu}{(n-1)^{-\nu/2}\,\sigfull^{\nu}}$}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2\nu+1}}\;.

Setting
C=\max\{C^{\prime\prime},C^{\prime\prime\prime}\}
and using a triangle inequality, we get the desired bound that

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{t\in\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}D_{n}>t\Big)-\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}{\sigfull}$}}W_{n}^{K}>t\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\;C\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(n-1)^{1/5}}$}}+\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n-1}\, \sigcond}{\sigfull}$}}\Big)^{2/5}+\,n^{-\frac{\nu-2}{4\nu+2}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mfullnu)^\nu}{\sigfull^{\nu} }$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{
(\Mcondnu)^\nu}{(n-1)^{-\nu/2}\,\sigfull^{\nu}}$}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2\nu+1}}\Big)\;.

\jmlrQED

D.2
Proofs for results on
W_{n}

Proof of Proposition
12

To prove the existence of distribution, we seek to apply Lévy’s continuity theorem. We first verify that there exists a sufficiently large
K^{*}
such that the sequence
(W_{n}^{K})_{K\geq K^{*}}
is tight. Since
2
holds for some
\nu\geq 2
, we get that as
K\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{K;2}\;\coloneqq\;\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})-u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\big|^{2}\big]^{1/2}\rightarrow 0\;.

In particular, there exists some sufficiently large
K^{*}
such that
\varepsilon_{K;2}\leq 1
for all
K\geq K^{*}
. By Lemma
33
, we have that for all
K\geq K^{*}
,

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[W_{n}^{K}]\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n(n-1)}$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}+\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n(n-1)}$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}+1)^{2}\;.

Note that by assumption, we have
|D|,\sigma_{\rm full}<\infty
. This implies that the sequence
(W_{n}^{K})_{K\geq K^{*}}
is tight by a Markov inequality:

\displaystyle\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}\Big(\sup\nolimits_{K\geq K^{*}}\mathbb{P}\big(\big|W_{n}^{K}\big|>x\big)\Big)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}\Big(x^{-2}\sup\nolimits_{K\geq K^{*}}\mathbb{E}[(W_{n}^{K})^{2}]\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 n^{-1}(n-1)^{-1} (\sigfull+ 1)^2 + D^2}{x^2}$}}\;=\;0\;.

We defer to Lemma
43
to show that the characteristic function of
(W_{n}^{K}-D)
converges pointwise as
K\rightarrow\infty
. This allows us to apply Lévy’s continuity theorem and obtain that
W_{n}
exists.

\jmlrQED

Proof of Lemma
13

The result holds by noting that for all
k>K^{*}
,
W_{n}^{K}=W_{n}^{K^{*}}
almost surely, and the latter random variable does not depend on
K
.

\jmlrQED

Lemma 43
.

The characteristic function of
(W_{n}^{K}-D)
converges pointwise as
K\rightarrow\infty
.

Proof of Lemma
43

Define
a_{k}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}\tau_{k;d}
and
T_{k}\coloneqq a_{k}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)
, which allows us to write

\displaystyle W_{n}^{K}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}$}}\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)+D\;=\;\msum_{k=1}^{K}T_{k}+D\;.

Denote
i=\sqrt{-1}
as the imaginary unit and
Y
as a chi-squared random variable with degree
1
. Since each
T_{k}
is a scaled and shifted chi-squared random variable with degree
1
, it has the characteristic function

\displaystyle\psi_{T_{k}}(t)\;=\;\mathbb{E}[\exp(it\,T_{k})]\;=\;\mathbb{E}[\exp(ia_{k}Yt)]\exp(-ia_{k}t)\;=

\displaystyle\;(1-2ia_{k}t)^{-1/2}\exp(-ia_{k}t)\;.

Since
T_{k}
’s are independent, by the convolution theorem, the characteristic function of
W_{n}^{K}-D
is given by

\displaystyle\psi_{W_{n}^{K}-D}(t)\;=\;\exp\Big(-i\msum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k}t\Big)\mprod_{k=1}^{K}(1-2ia_{k}t)^{-1/2}\;.

We want to prove that for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
\psi_{W_{n}^{K}-D}(t)
converges to some function as limit
K\rightarrow\infty
. By taking the principal-valued complex logarithm (i.e. discontinuity along negative real axis), we get that

\displaystyle\log\psi_{W_{n}^{K}-D}(t)\;=\;\msum_{k=1}^{K}\Big(-ia_{k}t-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\log(1-2ia_{k}t)\Big)+2im_{K}\pi\;\eqqcolon\;S_{K}+2im_{K}\pi\;,

(18)

for some
m_{K}\in\mathbb{N}
for each
K
that adjusts for values at discontinuity. Now consider the real part of the logarithm:

\displaystyle{\rm{Re}}\big(\log\psi_{W_{n}^{K}-D}(t)\big)\;=\;{\rm{Re}}(S_{K})\;=

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\msum_{k=1}^{K}\log|1-2ia_{k}t|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\msum_{k=1}^{K}\log\sqrt{1+4a_{k}^{2}t^{2}}\;=\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4}$}}\msum_{k=1}^{K}\log(1+4a_{k}^{2}t^{2})\;.

Recall by Lemma
32
that

\displaystyle\msum_{k=1}^{K}a_{k}^{2}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)}$}}\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{2}\;=\;\text{\rm Tr}((\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K})^{2})\xrightarrow{K\rightarrow\infty}\sigma_{\rm full}^{2}\;.

(19)

Fix
\epsilon>0
. The above implies that there exists a sufficiently large
K^{*}
such that for all
K_{1},K_{2}\geq K^{*}
,
\sum_{k=K_{1}}^{K_{2}}a_{k}^{2}<\epsilon
. Then for all
K_{1},K_{2}\geq K^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle 0\;\leq\;\msum_{k=K_{1}}^{K_{2}}\log(1+4a_{k}^{2}t^{2})\;\leq\;4t^{2}\msum_{k=K_{1}}^{K_{2}}a_{k}^{2}\;\leq\;4t^{2}\epsilon\;.

This implies that
({\rm{Re}}(S_{K}))_{K\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence and therefore converges. Now we handle the imaginary part. First let
m^{\prime}_{K}\in\mathbb{Z}
be such that

\displaystyle{{\rm{Im}}\Big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\log(1-2ia_{k}t)\Big)}\;=\;\msum_{k=1}^{K}\arctan(-2a_{k}t)+2m^{\prime}_{K}\pi\;.

Then we have

\displaystyle{\rm{Im}}(S_{K})\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{k=1}^{K}\Big(-a_{k}t+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\arctan(2a_{k}t)\Big)-m^{\prime}_{K}\pi\;\eqqcolon\;I_{K}-m^{\prime}_{K}\pi\;.

(20)

To show that
I_{K}
converges, we first note that by a third-order Taylor expansion, we have that
\arctan(x)=x+\frac{6(x_{*})^{2}-2}{6(x_{*}^{2}+1)^{3}}x^{3}
for some
x_{*}\in[0,x]
(we use this to denote
[0,x]
for
x\geq 0
as well as
[x,0]
for
x<0
, with an abuse of notation). This implies that for all
K_{1},K_{2}\geq K^{*}
, where
K^{*}
is defined as before,

\displaystyle\Big|\msum_{k=K_{1}}^{K_{2}}\Big(-a_{k}t+

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\arctan(2a_{k}t)\Big)\Big|\;=\;\Big|\msum_{k=K_{1}}^{K_{2}}\Big(-a_{k}t+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\arctan(2a_{k}t)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{k=K_{1}}^{K_{2}}\sup\nolimits_{b_{k}\in[0,a_{k}]}\Big|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{24 b_k^2t^2-2}{6(4b_k^2 t^2 + 1)^3}$}}8a_{k}^{3}t^{3}\Big|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;4t^{3}\msum_{k=K_{1}}^{K_{2}}|a_{k}|^{3}\,\Big(\sup\nolimits_{b_{k}\in[0,a_{k}]}\Big|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{24b_k^2t^2+6-8}{6(4b_k^2 t^2 + 1)^3}$}}\Big|\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;4t^{3}\msum_{k=K_{1}}^{K_{2}}|a_{k}|^{3}\,\Big(\sup\nolimits_{b_{k}\in[0,a_{k}]}\Big|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(4b_k^2 t^2 + 1)^2}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4}{3(4b_k^2 t^2 + 1)^2}$}}\Big|\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;20t^{3}\msum_{k=K_{1}}^{K_{2}}|a_{k}|^{3}\;\leq\;20t^{3}\Big(\msum_{k=K_{1}}^{K_{2}}(a_{k})^{2}\Big)^{3/2}\;\leq\;20t^{3}\epsilon^{3/2}\;,

where, in the last line, we have used the relative sizes of
l_{p}
norms. This implies that
I_{K}
converges. To show that
20
converges, we need to show that
m_{K}
in
20
is eventually constant. By using
20
and a triangle inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\pi|m^{\prime}_{K+1}-m^{\prime}_{K}|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;|I_{K+1}-I_{K}|+\Big|{\rm{Im}}(S_{K+1})-{\rm{Im}}(S_{K})\Big|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;|I_{K+1}-I_{K}|+\big|a_{K+1}t+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\log(1-2ia_{K+1}t)\big|\;.

The first term converges to zero, since we have shown that
I_{K}
converges. Since
a_{K}\rightarrow 0
by
19
and the complex logarithm we use is continuous outside
\{z:{\rm{Re}}(z)>0\}
, the second term above also converges to zero. Therefore
|m^{\prime}_{K+1}-m^{\prime}_{K}|\rightarrow 0
, and since
(m^{\prime}_{K})_{K\in\mathbb{N}}
is an integer sequence,
(m^{\prime}_{K})_{K\in\mathbb{N}}
converges. By
20
, this implies that
{\rm{Im}}(S_{K})
converges, and since we have shown
{\rm{Re}}(S_{K})
converges, we get that
S_{K}
converges. Finally, to show that
\psi_{W_{n}^{K}-D}(t)
converges, since
{\rm{Re}}(S_{K})={\rm{Re}}\big(\psi_{W_{n}^{K}-D}(t)\big)
, we only need to show that
{\rm{Im}}\big(\psi_{W_{n}^{K}-D}(t)\big)
converges. By
18
, this again reduces to showing that
m_{K}
is eventually constant. As before, by a triangle inequality,

\displaystyle 2\pi|m_{K+1}-m_{K}|\;\leq

\displaystyle\;|{\rm{Im}}(S_{K+1})-{\rm{Im}}(S_{K})|+\big|{\rm{Im}}(\log\psi_{W_{n}^{K+1}-D}(t))-{\rm{Im}}(\log\psi_{W_{n}^{K}-D}(t))\big|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;|{\rm{Im}}(S_{K+1})-{\rm{Im}}(S_{K})|+\big|a_{K+1}t+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\log(1-2ia_{K+1}t)\big|\;\xrightarrow{K\rightarrow\infty}0\;,

where the convergence of both terms has been shown earlier. This proves that the characteristic function
\psi_{W_{n}^{K}-D}(t)
converges for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
.

\jmlrQED

Appendix E
Proofs for
Section

4

E.1
Proofs for the general results

Proof of Lemma
15

To prove the first result, note that since
\kappa
is a kernel, there exists a RKHS
\mathcal{H}
and a map
\Phi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
such that we can write

\displaystyle u^{\rm MMD}\big((\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}),(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\big)\;=

\displaystyle\;\langle\Phi(\mathbf{x}),\Phi(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}+\langle\Phi(\mathbf{y}),\Phi(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}-\langle\Phi(\mathbf{x}),\Phi(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}-\langle\Phi(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),\Phi(\mathbf{y})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\langle\Phi(\mathbf{x})-\Phi(\mathbf{y}),\Phi(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})-\Phi(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\;.

Defining
\Phi_{*}\big((\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\big)\coloneqq\Phi(\mathbf{x})-\Phi(\mathbf{y})
proves that
u^{\rm MMD}
is a kernel. To prove the second result, note that by the definition of a weak Mercer representation, we have that almost surely

\displaystyle\big|\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{Z}_{1})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{Z}_{2})-u^{\rm MMD}(\mathbf{Z}_{1},\mathbf{Z}_{2})\big|\;\xrightarrow{K\rightarrow\infty}\;0\;,

which in particular implies convergence in probability. The argument uses the Vitali convergence theorem. By
3
, there exists some
\nu^{*}>\nu
such that
\sup_{K\geq 1}\mathbb{E}[|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{Z}_{1})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{Z}_{2})|^{\nu^{*}}]<\infty
and
\mathbb{E}[|u^{\rm MMD}(\mathbf{Z}_{1},\mathbf{Z}_{2})|^{\nu^{*}}]<\infty
. By a triangle inequality and a Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\;\sup\nolimits_{K\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big|\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{Z}_{1})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{Z}_{2})-u^{\rm MMD}(\mathbf{Z}_{1},\mathbf{Z}_{2})\Big|^{\nu^{*}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\sup\nolimits_{K\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big|\,\big|\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{Z}_{1})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{Z}_{2})\big|+\big|u^{\rm MMD}(\mathbf{Z}_{1},\mathbf{Z}_{2})\big|\,\Big|^{\nu^{*}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;2^{\nu^{*}-1}\sup\nolimits_{K\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{Z}_{1})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{Z}_{2})\big|^{\nu^{*}}\Big]+2^{\nu^{*}-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|u^{\rm MMD}(\mathbf{Z}_{1},\mathbf{Z}_{2})\big|^{\nu^{*}}\Big]\;<\;\infty\;.

This implies for any
\nu\in(2,\nu^{*})
, the sequence
\big(\big(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{Z}_{1})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{Z}_{2})-u^{\rm MMD}(\mathbf{Z}_{1},\mathbf{Z}_{2})\big)^{\nu}\big)_{K\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly integrable, and therefore converges to zero in
L_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2d},P\otimes Q)
by the Vitali convergence theorem. Since convergence in
L_{\nu}
implies convergence in
L_{\min\{\nu,3\}}
, we get that
2
holds for
\min\{\nu,3\}
.

\jmlrQED

Before we prove the next result, recall that
\{\lambda_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
and
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
are defined as the weak Mercer representation for the kernel
\kappa
under
Q
, and we have assumed that
\phi_{k}
’s are differentiable. We have also defined the sequence of values
\{\alpha_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
and the sequence of functions
\{\psi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
in (
6
) as

\displaystyle\alpha_{(k^{\prime}-1)d+l}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\lambda_{k^{\prime}}\;

and

\displaystyle\psi_{(k^{\prime}-1)d+l}(\mathbf{x})\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;(\partial_{x_{l}}\log p(\mathbf{x}))\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})+\partial_{x_{l}}\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})\;,

for
1\leq l\leq d
and
k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
. For convenience, we denote
\psi_{k^{\prime};l}\coloneqq\psi_{(k^{\prime}-1)d+l}
in the proof below.

Proof of Lemma
16

Recall that
\psi_{k^{\prime};l}(\mathbf{x})\coloneqq(\partial_{x_{l}}\log p(\mathbf{x}))\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})+\partial_{x_{l}}\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})
. Write
\tilde{\psi}_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})\coloneqq(\psi_{k^{\prime};1}(\mathbf{x}),\ldots,\psi_{k^{\prime};n}(\mathbf{x}))^{\top}
. We first consider the error term with
dK^{\prime}
summands for some
K^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{k=1}^{dK^{\prime}}\alpha_{k}\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})-u_{P}^{\rm KSD}(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\Big|^{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{l=1}^{d}\msum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{K^{\prime}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}}\psi_{k^{\prime};l}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\psi_{k^{\prime};l}(\mathbf{X}_{2})-u_{P}^{\rm KSD}(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\Big|^{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{K^{\prime}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}}\big(\tilde{\psi}_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\big)^{\top}\big(\tilde{\psi}_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)-u_{P}^{\rm KSD}(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\Big|^{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[\big|T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}+T_{4}-u_{P}^{\rm KSD}(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\big|^{\nu}\big]\;,

where the random quantities are defined in terms of
\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2}\overset{i.i.d.}{\sim}Q
:

\displaystyle T_{1}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1})\big)^{\top}\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)\msum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{K^{\prime}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}}\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\;,

\displaystyle T_{2}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1})\big)^{\top}\Big(\msum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{K^{\prime}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}}\big(\nabla\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\Big)\;,

\displaystyle T_{3}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)^{\top}\Big(\msum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{K^{\prime}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}}\big(\nabla\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\big)\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\Big)\;,

\displaystyle T_{4}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{K^{\prime}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}}\big(\nabla\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\big)^{\top}\big(\nabla\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)\;.

Recall that by
3
, there exists some
\nu^{*}>\nu
such that we have
\|\kappa^{*}(\mathbf{Z}_{1},\mathbf{Z}_{2})\|_{L_{\nu^{*}}}<\infty
and
\sup_{K\geq 1}\|\sum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{Z}_{1})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{Z}_{2})\|_{L_{\nu^{*}}}<\infty
. By using the proof of the second part of Lemma
15
above, for
\nu^{\Delta}\coloneqq\frac{\nu+\nu^{*}}{2}\in(\nu,\nu^{*})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\msum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{K^{\prime}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}}\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{2})-u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\big|^{\nu^{\Delta}}\Big]\;\xrightarrow{K^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty}\;0\;.

Meanwhile by
4
,

\big\|\|\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1})\|_{2}\big\|_{L_{2{\nu^{**}}}}<\infty
, where

\displaystyle\nu^{**}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\nu(\nu+\nu^*)}{\nu^* - \nu}$}}\;=\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\nu}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{\nu+ \nu^*}$}}\Big)^{-1}\;=\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\nu}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\nu^{\Delta}}$}}\Big)^{-1}\;>\;\nu\;.

By a Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and a Hölder’s inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\big\|\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1})\big)^{\top}\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)\big\|_{L_{\nu^{**}}}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\big\|\,\|\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1})\|_{2}\,\big\|_{L_{2\nu^{**}}}<\infty\;.

Now by a Hölder’s inequality and noting that
(\nu^{**})^{-1}+(\nu^{\Delta})^{-1}=\nu^{-1}
, we can now bound the error of using
T_{1}
to approximate the first term of
u_{P}^{\rm KSD}
as

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[|E_{1}|^{\nu}]\;\coloneqq\;\mathbb{E}\big[\big|T_{1}-\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1})\big)^{\top}\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)\,u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\big|^{\nu}\big]

\displaystyle=\;\big\|T_{1}-\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1})\big)^{\top}\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)\,u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\big\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\leq\;\big\|\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1})\big)^{\top}\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)\big\|_{L_{\nu^{**}}}^{\nu}\,\big\|\msum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{K^{\prime}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}}\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{2})-u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\big\|_{L_{\nu^{\Delta}}}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{K^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty}\;0\;.

For
T_{2}
, we consider a similar approximation error quantity and apply a Cauchy-Schwarz inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|E_{2}|^{\nu}]\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[\big|T_{2}-\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1})\big)^{\top}\nabla_{2}\kappa(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\big|^{\nu}\big]

\displaystyle=\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1})\big)^{\top}\Big(\msum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{K^{\prime}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}}\big(\nabla\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{1})-\nabla_{2}\kappa(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\Big)\Big|^{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\;\|\,\|\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1})\|_{2}\,\|_{L_{2\nu}}^{\nu}\Big\|\Big\|\msum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{K^{\prime}}\lambda_{k^{\prime}}\big(\nabla\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)\phi_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbf{X}_{1})-\nabla_{2}\kappa(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\Big\|_{2}\Big\|_{L_{2\nu}}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{K^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty}\;0\;,

where we have noted that the first term is bounded since
2\nu<2\nu^{**}
and used
4
(iv). By symmetry of
\kappa
and the fact that
\mathbf{X}_{1}
and
\mathbf{X}_{2}
are exchangeable, we have the same result for
T_{3}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|E_{3}|^{\nu}]\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[\big|T_{3}-\big(\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)^{\top}\nabla_{1}\kappa(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\big|^{\nu}\big]\;\xrightarrow{K^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty}\;0\;.

Meanwhile, the second condition of
4
(iv) directly says that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|E_{4}|^{\nu}]\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[\big|T_{4}-\text{\rm Tr}\big(\nabla_{1}\nabla_{2}\kappa(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)\big|^{\nu}\big]\;\xrightarrow{K^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty}\;0\;.

Combining the results and applying a Jensen’s inequality to the convex function
x\mapsto|x|^{\nu}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{k=1}^{dK^{\prime}}\alpha_{k}\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})-

\displaystyle u_{P}^{\rm KSD}(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\Big|^{\nu}\Big]\;=\;\mathbb{E}\big[\big|E_{1}+E_{2}+E_{3}+E_{4}\big|^{\nu}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4}$}}(4E_{1})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4}$}}(4E_{2})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4}$}}(4E_{3})+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4}$}}(4E_{4})\big|^{\nu}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4^{\nu-1}\big(\mathbb{E}[|E_{1}|^{\nu}]+\mathbb{E}[|E_{2}|^{\nu}]+\mathbb{E}[|E_{3}|^{\nu}]+\mathbb{E}[|E_{4}|^{\nu}]\big)\;\xrightarrow{K^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty}\;0\;.

Now consider
K\in\mathbb{N}
that is not necessarily divisible by
d
, and let
K^{\prime}
be the greatest integer such that
K\geq dK^{\prime}
. Then by a triangle inequality and a similar Jensen’s inequality as above, we get

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{k=1}^{K}\alpha_{k}\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})-u_{P}^{\rm KSD}(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\Big|^{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;2^{\nu-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{k=1}^{dK^{\prime}}\alpha_{k}\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})-u_{P}^{\rm KSD}(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\Big|^{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle\;+2^{\nu-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{k=dK^{\prime}+1}^{K}\alpha_{k}\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\Big|^{\nu}\Big]\;.

(21)

The first term is
o(1)
as
K\rightarrow\infty
by the previous argument, so we only need to focus on the second term. The expectation can be bounded by noting that
\alpha_{k}=\lambda_{K^{\prime}+1}\geq 0
for all
dK^{\prime}+1\leq k\leq K
and using a triangle inequality followed by a Jensen’s inequality:

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{k=dK^{\prime}+1}^{K}\alpha_{k}\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\Big|^{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;(\lambda_{K^{\prime}+1})^{\nu}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{K-dK'}$}}\msum_{k=dK^{\prime}+1}^{K}(K-dK^{\prime})|\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})|\Big)^{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;(\lambda_{K^{\prime}+1})^{\nu}(K-dK^{\prime})^{\nu-1}\msum_{k=dK^{\prime}+1}^{K}\mathbb{E}[|\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})|^{\nu}]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;(\lambda_{K^{\prime}+1})^{\nu}d^{\nu}\sup\nolimits_{k\in\{dK^{\prime}+1,\ldots,dK^{\prime}+d\}}\mathbb{E}[|\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})|^{\nu}]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;(\lambda_{K^{\prime}+1})^{\nu}d^{\nu}\sup\nolimits_{1\leq l\leq d}\mathbb{E}[|\psi_{dK^{\prime}+l}(\mathbf{X}_{1})|^{\nu}]^{2}\;.

In the last equality, we have noted that
\mathbf{X}_{1}
and
\mathbf{X}_{2}
are identically distributed. Now by the definition of
\psi_{k}
, another Jensen’s inequality on
x\mapsto|x|^{\nu}
and a Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\psi_{dK^{\prime}+l}(\mathbf{X}_{1})

\displaystyle|^{\nu}]\;=\;\mathbb{E}[|(\partial_{x_{l}}\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1}))\phi_{K^{\prime}+1}(\mathbf{X}_{1})+\partial_{x_{l}}\phi_{K^{\prime}+1}(\mathbf{X}_{1})|^{\nu}]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;2^{\nu-1}\mathbb{E}[|(\partial_{x_{l}}\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1}))\phi_{K^{\prime}+1}(\mathbf{X}_{1})|^{\nu}]+2^{\nu-1}\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{x_{l}}\phi_{K^{\prime}+1}(\mathbf{X}_{1})|^{\nu}]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;2^{\nu-1}\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{x_{l}}\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1})|^{2\nu}]^{1/2}\,\mathbb{E}[|\phi_{K^{\prime}+1}(\mathbf{X}_{1})|^{2\nu}]^{1/2}+2^{\nu-1}\mathbb{E}[|\partial_{x_{l}}\phi_{K^{\prime}+1}(\mathbf{X}_{1})|^{\nu}]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;2^{\nu-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1})\|^{2\nu}_{2}]^{1/2}\,\mathbb{E}[|\phi_{K^{\prime}+1}(\mathbf{X}_{1})|^{2\nu}]^{1/2}+2^{\nu-1}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla\phi_{K^{\prime}+1}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\|_{2}^{\nu}]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;2^{\nu-1}\|\|\nabla\log p(\mathbf{X}_{1})\|_{2}\|_{L_{2\nu}}^{\nu}\,\|\phi_{K^{\prime}+1}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\|_{L_{2\nu}}^{\nu}+2^{\nu-1}\|\|\nabla\phi_{K^{\prime}+1}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\|_{2}\|_{L_{\nu}}^{\nu}\;.

By
4
(i), (ii) and (iii), all three norms are bounded, so
\mathbb{E}[|\psi_{dK^{\prime}+l}(\mathbf{X}_{1})|^{\nu}]<\infty
. By the definition of
\lambda_{k}
from the weak Mercer representation, as
K\rightarrow\infty
and therefore
K^{\prime}\rightarrow\infty
,
\lambda_{K^{\prime}+1}\rightarrow 0
, which implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{k=dK^{\prime}+1}^{K}\alpha_{k}\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\Big|^{\nu}\Big]\;=\;o(1)\;.

This means that both terms in (
21
) converge to
0
as
K\rightarrow\infty
. In other words,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\msum_{k=1}^{K}

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\psi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})-u_{P}^{\rm KSD}(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})\Big|^{\nu}\Big]\;\xrightarrow{K\rightarrow\infty}\;0\;.

Since
L_{\nu}
-convergence implies
L_{\min\{\nu,3\}}
-convergence and we have assumed that
\nu>2
, we get that
2
holds for
\min\{\nu,3\}
with respect to the
u_{P}^{\rm KSD}
,
\alpha_{k}
and
\psi_{k}
.

\jmlrQED

E.2
Proof of Proposition
18

From Lemma
22
, we can write the variance ratio as

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigfull^2}{\sigcond^2}$}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\gamma}{4 + \gamma}$}}\right)^{d/2}\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(1 + \gamma) (3 + \gamma)}{ \gamma^2 }$}}\right)^{d/2}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B}{A}$}}\;=\;C\times\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{B}{A}$}}\;,

where

\displaystyle A\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(2 + \gamma)^2}{(1 + \gamma)(3 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\Big(1-\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(1 + \gamma)(3 + \gamma)}{(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}\right)^{d/2}\Big)\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\left(1+o(1)\right)\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\Big(1-\left(1-\alpha\right)^{d/2}\Big)\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4}

\displaystyle B\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;d+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^2}{\gamma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2d \| \bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma}$}}+2\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\Big(1-\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\gamma(4 + \gamma)}{(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}\right)^{d/2}\Big)\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4}+o\Big(d+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^2}{\gamma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d \| \bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma}$}}+\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;d+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^2}{\gamma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2d \| \bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma}$}}+2\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\Big(1-\left(1-\delta\right)^{d/2}\Big)\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4}+o\Big(d+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^2}{\gamma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d \| \bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma}$}}+\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle C\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\gamma}{4 + \gamma}$}}\Big)^{d/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(1 + \gamma) (3 + \gamma)}{ \gamma^2 }$}}\Big)^{d/2}\;=\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(1 + \gamma) (3 + \gamma)}{ \gamma(4 + \gamma) }$}}\Big)^{d/2}\;,

and we have written
\frac{(1+\gamma)(3+\gamma)}{(2+\gamma)^{2}}=1-\alpha
with
\alpha\coloneqq\frac{1}{(2+\gamma)^{2}}
and
\frac{\gamma(4+\gamma)}{(2+\gamma)^{2}}=1-\delta
with
\delta\coloneqq\frac{4}{(2+\gamma)^{2}}
. To simplify
A
and
B
, we first rewrite

\displaystyle 1-\left(1-\alpha\right)^{d/2}\;=\;1-\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2}$}}\log\left(1-\alpha\right)\right)\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;1-\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2}$}}\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;1-\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\;.

(22)

In
(a)
, we have used a Taylor expansion by noting that
\gamma
is small by the stated assumption
\gamma=\omega(1)
. Similarly we can obtain

\displaystyle 1-(1-\delta)^{d/2}\;=\;1-\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2}$}}\log(1-\delta)\right)\;=\;1-\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2}$}}\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4}{(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\;\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;\;\;\;\;=\;1-\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2d}{(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\;,

(23)

\displaystyle\;C\;=\;\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2}$}}\log\left(1+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3}{\gamma(4 + \gamma)}$}}\right)\right)\;=\;\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2}$}}\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3}{\gamma(4 + \gamma)}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;=\;\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{2\gamma(4 + \gamma)}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\;.

(24)

Therefore, the terms
(1-\alpha)^{d/2}
,
(1-\gamma)^{d/2}
and
C
can be small, large or close to a constant, depending on whether
\gamma^{2}
grows faster than, lower than, or at the same rate as
d
. We now consider the three cases individually.

Case 1:
\gamma=o(d^{1/2})
.

In this case,
\frac{d}{(2+\gamma)^{2}}=\omega(1)
, so we have
(1-\alpha)^{d/2}=o(1)
and
(1-\delta)^{d/2}=o(1)
. Therefore

\displaystyle A\;=

\displaystyle\;(1+o(1))\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+(1+o(1))\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4}\;=\;\Theta(\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4})\;=\;\Theta(\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4})\;,

and

\displaystyle B\;=

\displaystyle\;d+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^2}{\gamma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2d \| \bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma}$}}+2\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4}+o\left(d+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^2}{\gamma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d \| \bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma}$}}+\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Theta\left(d+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^2}{\gamma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d \| \bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma}$}}+\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4}\right)\;.

Combining with the previous expressions for
A
,
B
and
C
yields

\displaystyle\rho_{d}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigfull}{\sigcond}$}}\;=\;\sqrt{C}\times\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{B}}{\sqrt{A}}$}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{4\gamma(4 + \gamma)}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\Theta\bigg(\sqrt{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \| \bmu\|_2^{4}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^2}{\gamma^2 \| \bmu\|_2^4}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma\| \bmu\|_2^2}$}}+1}\;\bigg)

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{4\gamma^2}$}}+o\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^2}$}}\right)\right)\Theta\bigg(\sqrt{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^2}{\gamma^2 \| \bmu\|_2^{4}}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma\| \bmu\|_2^2}$}}+1}\;\bigg)

\displaystyle\;\overset{(b)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{4\gamma^2}$}}+o\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^2}$}}\right)\right)\Theta\bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma\| \bmu\|_2^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^{1/2}}{\gamma^{1/2} \| \bmu\|_2}$}}+1\bigg)\;,

where in
(a)
we have used the fact that
\gamma=o(d^{1/2})
, and in
(b)
we have noted that for
a,b,c>0
,
\sqrt{a+b+c}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}+\sqrt{c}
and by a Jensen’s inequality,
\sqrt{a+b+c}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}+\sqrt{c})
.

Case 2:
\gamma=\omega(d^{1/2})
.

Since in this case
\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}}
is small, we can use Taylor expansion to approximate the exponential term in (
22
) to get

\displaystyle 1-\left(1-\alpha\right)^{d/2}\;=\;1-\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\;=

\displaystyle\;1-\left(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2 (2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^2}{\gamma^{4}}$}}\Big)\right)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2 (2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+o\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^2}$}}\Big)\;.

Using a similar argument applied to (
23
), we have

\displaystyle 1-\left(1-\delta\right)^{d/2}\;=\;1-\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2d}{(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2d}{(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+o\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^2}$}}\Big)\;,

and (
24
) yields

\displaystyle C\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{2\gamma(4 + \gamma)}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\;=\;1+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{2\gamma(4 + \gamma)}$}}+O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^2}{\gamma^{4}}$}}\Big)\;=\;1+o(1)\;.

We therefore conclude that

\displaystyle A\;=

\displaystyle\;(1+o(1))\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+o\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^2}$}}\Big)\,\Big)\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4}\;=\;\Theta\left(\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^2}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4}\right)\;,

A similar argument shows that

\displaystyle B\;=

\displaystyle\;d+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^2}{\gamma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2d \| \bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma}$}}+2\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4}\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2d}{ (2 + \gamma)^2 }$}}+o\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^2}$}}\right)\right)+o\left(d+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^2}{\gamma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d \| \bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma}$}}+\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Theta\left(d+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d \| \bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma}$}}+\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d \| \bmu\|_2^4}{ \gamma^2 }$}}\right)\;,

where in the last line we noted that
\gamma=\omega(d^{1/2})
implies
\frac{d^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}=o(d)
. Combining the results gives

\displaystyle\rho_{d}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigfull}{\sigcond}$}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\sqrt{C}\times\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{B}}{\sqrt{A}}$}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\sqrt{1+o(1)}\;\;\Theta\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^{1/2} + \gamma^{-1/2}d^{1/2} \|\mu\|_2 + \|\mu\|_2 + \gamma^{-1} d^{1/2} \|\mu\|_2^2 }{ \|\mu\|_2 + \gamma^{-1} d^{1/2} \|\mu\|_2^2}$}}\right)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Theta\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^{1/2} + \gamma^{-1/2}d^{1/2} \|\mu\|_2}{\|\mu\|_2 + \gamma^{-1} d^{1/2} \|\mu\|_2^2}$}}+1\Big)\;=\;\Theta\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^{1/2} (1 + \gamma^{-1/2} \|\mu\|_2)}{\|\mu\|_2 \, ( 1 + \gamma^{-1} d^{1/2} \|\mu\|_2)}$}}+1\Big)\;.

Case 3:
\gamma=\Theta(d^{1/2})
.

Since in this case
\frac{d}{\gamma^{4}}
is small, we have that
\exp\big(O\big(\frac{d}{\gamma^{4}}\big)\big)=1+O\big(\frac{d}{\gamma^{4}}\big)
by a Taylor expansion. Substituting this into (
22
), we have

\displaystyle 0\;\leq\;1-\left(1-\alpha\right)^{d/2}\;=

\displaystyle\;1-\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}\right)\left(1+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;1-\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}\right)+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\;=\;\Theta(1)\;,

where the last line holds as
1-\exp\big(-\frac{d}{2(2+\gamma)^{2}}\big)=\Theta(1)
. A similar argument applied to (
23
) and (
24
) gives

\displaystyle 0\;\leq\;1-\left(1-\delta\right)^{d/2}\;=

\displaystyle\;1-\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2d}{(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\;=\;\Theta(1)\;,

\displaystyle 0\;\leq\;C\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{2\gamma(4 + \gamma)}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\;=\;\Theta(1)\;.

Combining the above derivations yields

\displaystyle A\;=

\displaystyle\;\Theta(\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4})\;=\;\Theta(\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4})\;,

\displaystyle B\;=

\displaystyle\;\Theta\Big(d+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^2}{\gamma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2d \| \bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma}$}}+2\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4}\Big)\;=\;\Theta\Big(d+d^{1/2}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4}\Big)\;,

where in the equality for
B
we have used the fact that
\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}=\Omega(1)
implies
\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}=O(\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4})
and that
\gamma=\Theta(d^{1/2})
implies
\frac{d}{\gamma}=\Theta(d^{1/2})
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\rho_{d}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigfull}{\sigcond}$}}\;=\;\sqrt{C}\times\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{B}}{\sqrt{A}}$}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\Theta\left(\sqrt{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\| \bmu\|_2^4}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^{1/2}}{ \| \bmu\|_2^2}$}}+1}\;\right)\;=\;\Theta\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^{1/2}}{\| \bmu\|_2^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d^{1/4}}{ \| \bmu\|_2}$}}+1\right)\;.

This completes the proof.

E.3
Proof of Proposition
19

Recall the expressions of
\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}
and
\sigma_{\rm full}^{2}
for MMD-RBF from Lemma
24
, which allow us to rewrite
\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}=CA
and
\sigma_{\rm full}^{2}=CB
, where

\displaystyle A\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;1+\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{3 + \gamma}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\right)+2\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3 + \gamma}{2 + \gamma}$}}\right)^{d/2}\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 + \gamma}{2 + \gamma}$}}\right)^{d/2}\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(2 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\;-2\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 + \gamma}{2(1 + \gamma)(3 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\right)-\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3 + \gamma}{2 + \gamma}$}}\right)^{d/2}\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 + \gamma}{2 + \gamma}$}}\right)^{d/2}

\displaystyle\;-\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3 + \gamma}{2 + \gamma}$}}\right)^{d/2}\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 + \gamma}{2 + \gamma}$}}\right)^{d/2}\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 + \gamma}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle B\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3+\gamma}{4+\gamma}$}}\Big)^{d/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1+\gamma}{\gamma}$}}\Big)^{d/2}\Big(1+\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4+\gamma}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big)-\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3+\gamma}{2+\gamma}$}}\Big)^{d/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1+\gamma}{2+\gamma}$}}\Big)^{d/2}

\displaystyle\;-4\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2+\gamma}{2(1+\gamma)(3+\gamma)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)-\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3+\gamma}{2+\gamma}$}}\Big)^{d/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1+\gamma}{2+\gamma}$}}\Big)^{d/2}\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2+\gamma}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\;+4\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3+\gamma}{2+\gamma}$}}\Big)^{d/2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1+\gamma}{2+\gamma}$}}\Big)^{d/2}\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(2+\gamma)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle C\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;2\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\gamma}{1 + \gamma}$}}\right)^{d/2}\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\gamma}{3 + \gamma}$}}\right)^{d/2}\;.

This implies that
\sigma_{\rm full}^{2}/\sigma_{\rm cond}^{2}=B/A
, so it suffices to calculate the leading terms in
A
and
B
, respectively. We first write
\frac{(3+\gamma)(1+\gamma)}{(2+\gamma)^{2}}=1-\frac{1}{(2+\gamma)^{2}}\eqqcolon 1-\alpha
and
\frac{(3+\gamma)(1+\gamma)}{(4+\gamma)\gamma}=1+\frac{3}{\gamma(4+\gamma)}=:1+\beta
, where
\alpha
and
\beta
are small as
\gamma=\omega(1)
by assumption. Rearranging
A
gives

\displaystyle A\;=

\displaystyle\;1+\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{3 + \gamma}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\right)-2\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 + \gamma}{2(1 + \gamma)(3 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\;+(1-\alpha)^{d/2}\Big(2\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(2 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)-1-\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 + \gamma}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;A_{1}+(1-\alpha)^{d/2}A_{2}\;,

and similarly,

\displaystyle B\;=

\displaystyle\;(1+\beta)^{d/2}\Big(1+\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4+\gamma}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big)-4\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2+\gamma}{2(1+\gamma)(3+\gamma)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\;-(1-\alpha)^{d/2}\Big(1+\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2+\gamma}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)-4\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(2+\gamma)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;(1+\beta)^{d/2}B_{1}-B_{2}-(1-\alpha)^{d/2}B_{3}\;.

These expressions can be simplified further depending on the relative growth rates of
d,\gamma
and
\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}
; we consider these cases individually.

Case 1:
\gamma=o(d^{1/2})
and
\gamma=o(\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2})
.

Since
\gamma=o(\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2})
, all exponential terms of the form
\exp\left(-\frac{1}{a(b+\gamma)}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\right)
, for any positive constants
a,b
, are
o(1)
. Moreover, since we have assumed that
\gamma=\omega(1)
, we can apply a Taylor expansion to yield

\displaystyle(1-\alpha)^{d/2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2}$}}\log\left(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2}$}}\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\right)\;=\;\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2 \gamma^2}$}}+o\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{2}}$}}\right)\right)\;.

(25)

Therefore, when
\gamma=o(d^{1/2})
, we have
(1-\alpha)^{d/2}=o(1)
. Thus the dominating term in
A
is the leading constant
1
and

\displaystyle A\;=\;1+o(1)\;.

To control
B
, we first consider a similar Taylor expansion by noting that
\gamma=\omega(1)
:

\displaystyle(1+\beta)^{d/2}\;=\;\exp\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2}$}}\log\Big(1+

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3}{\gamma(4 + \gamma)}$}}\Big)\Big)\;=\;\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2}$}}\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3}{\gamma( 4 + \gamma)}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{\gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{2\gamma( 4 + \gamma)}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{4}}$}}\right)\right)\;=\;\exp\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{2\gamma^2}$}}+o\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{2}}$}}\right)\Big)\;.

(26)

Since
\gamma=o(d^{1/2})
, we have that
(1+\beta)^{d/2}=\omega(1)
. All exponential terms and
(1-\alpha)^{d/2}
are
o(1)
by the calculations above, so

\displaystyle B\;=\;(1+\beta)^{d/2}+o((1+\beta)^{d/2})\;=\;\Theta\Big(\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{2\gamma^2}$}}+o\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{2}}$}}\right)\right)\Big)\;.

Combining the results for
A
and
B
gives

\displaystyle\rho_{d}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigfull}{\sigcond}$}}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{B}}{\sqrt{A}}$}}\;=\;\Theta\Big(\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{4\gamma^2}$}}+o\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{2}}$}}\right)\right)\Big)\;.

Case 2:
\gamma=o(d^{1/2})
and
\gamma=\omega(\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2})
.

Since
\gamma=\omega(\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2})
, we can bound
A_{1}
by first extracting an exponential factor and then applying two second-order Taylor expansions:

\displaystyle A_{1}\;=\;

\displaystyle\;1+\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{3 + \gamma}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\right)-2\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 + \gamma}{2(1 + \gamma)(3 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;1+\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{3 + \gamma}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\Big(1-2\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\gamma}{2(1 + \gamma)(3 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;1+\Big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \| \bmu\|_2^2 }{3 + \gamma}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^4}{2(3 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^6}{\gamma^3}$}}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\;\qquad\qquad\times\Big(-1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\gamma\| \bmu\|_2^2}{(1 + \gamma)(3 + \gamma)}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\gamma^2 \| \bmu\|_2^4}{4(1 + \gamma)^2(3 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^6}{ \gamma^3}$}}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;1-1+\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{3 + \gamma}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\gamma}{(1 + \gamma)(3 + \gamma)}$}}\Big)\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\;+\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(3+\gamma)^2}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\gamma^2}{4(1+\gamma)^2(3+\gamma)^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\gamma}{(1+\gamma)(3+\gamma)^2}$}}\Big)\|\mu\|_{2}^{4}+O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^6}{\gamma^3}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(1 + \gamma) (3 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{-2+\gamma^2}{4(3+4\gamma+\gamma^2)^2}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4}+O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^6}{\gamma^3}$}}\Big)\;.

Note that the first term is on the order
\gamma^{-2}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}
, the second term is on the order
\gamma^{-2}\|\mu\|_{2}^{4}
and the third term is on the order
\gamma^{-3}\|\mu\|_{2}^{6}
. Since
\gamma^{-1}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}=o(1)
and
\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}=\Omega(1)
, the second term dominates and we get that

\displaystyle A_{1}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\bmu\|_2^4}{4\gamma^2}$}}+o\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\bmu\|_2^4 }{\gamma^2}$}}\Big)\;.

(27)

To control
A_{2}
, we use a similar Taylor expansion to get that

\displaystyle A_{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;2\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(2 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\right)-1-\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 + \gamma}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;-1+\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 + \gamma}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)\;\Big(2\exp\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(2 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)-1\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;-1+\Big(1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^2 }{2 + \gamma}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^4 }{2(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^6 }{\gamma^3}$}}\Big)\,\Big)\;\Big(1+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^2 }{2 + \gamma}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^4 }{4(2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^6 }{\gamma^3}$}}\Big)\,\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4(2+\gamma)^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(2+\gamma)^2}$}}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(2+\gamma)^2}$}}\Big)\|\mu\|_{2}^{4}+O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^6 }{\gamma^3}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\bmu\|_2^4}{4(2+\gamma)^2}$}}+O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^6 }{\gamma^3}$}}\Big)\;.

(28)

In particular, we have
A_{2}=O(\gamma^{-2}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{4})=O(A_{1})
. Since
\gamma=o(d^{1/2})
, we have
(1-\alpha)^{d/2}=o(1)
as before, which implies

\displaystyle A\;=\;A_{1}+(1-\alpha)^{d/2}A_{2}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\bmu\|_2^4}{4\gamma^2}$}}+o\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\bmu\|_2^4 }{\gamma^2}$}}\Big)\;.

To control
B
, recall we have shown in Case 1 that
(1+\beta)^{d/2}=\omega(1)
and
(1-\alpha)^{d/2}=o(1)
for
\gamma=o(d^{1/2})
. All exponential terms are
O(1)
and
B_{1}=2+O(\gamma^{-1}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2})
by a Taylor expansion. By (
26
), we obtain that

\displaystyle B\;=\;(1+\beta)^{d/2}B_{1}-B_{2}-(1-\alpha)^{d/2}B_{3}\;=

\displaystyle\;2(1+\beta)^{d/2}+o((1+\beta)^{d/2})

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Theta\Big(\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{2\gamma^2}$}}+o\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{2}}$}}\right)\right)\Big)\;.

We hence conclude that

\displaystyle\rho_{d}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigfull}{\sigcond}$}}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{B}}{\sqrt{A}}$}}\;=

\displaystyle\;\Theta\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\gamma}{\|\mu\|_2^2}$}}\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{4\gamma^2}$}}+o\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{2}}$}}\right)\right)\Big)\;.

Case 3:
\gamma=\omega(\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2})
and
\gamma=\omega(d^{1/2})
.

We first rewrite the expressions of
A
and
B
as

\displaystyle A\;=

\displaystyle\;(A_{1}+A_{2})-(1-(1-\alpha)^{d/2})A_{2}\;,

(29)

\displaystyle B\;=

\displaystyle\;(B_{1}-B_{2}-B_{3})+((1+\beta)^{d/2}-1)B_{1}+(1-(1-\alpha)^{d/2})B_{3}\;.

(30)

Since
\gamma=\omega(d^{1/2})
, we can perform a further Taylor expansion on the expressions in (
25
) and (
26
):

\displaystyle(1-\alpha)^{d/2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2(2+\gamma)^2}$}}+O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^4}$}}\Big)\right)\;=\;1-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2 (2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{4} }$}}\right)\;,

(31)

\displaystyle(1+\beta)^{d/2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{2\gamma(4+\gamma)}$}}+O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{4}}$}}\Big)\right)\;=\;1+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{2\gamma(4 + \gamma)}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{4} }$}}\right)\;,

(32)

On the other hand, since
\gamma^{-1}\|\mu\|^{2}_{2}
is small, we can consider performing Taylor expansions on each exponential. By grouping the terms and extracting an appropriate exponential, we get that

\displaystyle A_{1}+A_{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{3 + \gamma}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\right)-\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 + \gamma}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\;-2\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 + \gamma}{2(1 + \gamma)(3 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\right)+2\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(2 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2 + \gamma}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\Big(\exp\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(3+\gamma)(2 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)-1\Big)

\displaystyle\;-2\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(2 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big(-\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(6+11\gamma+6\gamma^2+\gamma^3)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\right)+1\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(3+\gamma)(2 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}+o\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{(3+\gamma)(2 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)+O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma^{3}}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\mu\|_2^2 }{(3+\gamma)(2 + \gamma)}$}}+o\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^2}{ \gamma^{2}}$}}\Big)\;.

(33)

In the last line, we have used that the dominating term is of the order
\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}/\gamma^{2}
. For
A_{2}
, we recall from (
28
) that
A_{2}=-\frac{\|\mu\|_{2}^{4}}{4\gamma^{2}}+o\big(\frac{\|\mu\|_{2}^{4}}{\gamma^{2}}\big)
. Substituting the computations into (
29
) and using (
31
), we obtain that

\displaystyle A\;=

\displaystyle\;(A_{1}+A_{2})-(1-(1-\alpha)^{d/2})A_{2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\mu\|_2^2 }{(3+\gamma)(2 + \gamma)}$}}+o\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\| \bmu\|_2^2}{ \gamma^{2}}$}}\Big)+\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2(2+\gamma)^2}$}}+O\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^4}$}}\Big)\Big)\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\mu\|_2^4}{4(2+\gamma)^2}$}}+o\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\mu\|_2^4}{\gamma^2}$}}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Theta\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\mu\|_2^2}{\gamma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d\|\mu\|_2^4 }{\gamma^4}$}}\Big)\;.

We use a similar argument to compute
B
. By grouping terms appropriately and performing Taylor expansions, we have

\displaystyle B_{1}-B_{2}-B_{3}\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4+\gamma}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)-\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2+\gamma}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\;-4\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2+\gamma}{2(1+\gamma)(3+\gamma)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)+4\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(2+\gamma)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4+\gamma}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big(1-\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{(4+\gamma)(2+\gamma)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\;-4\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(2 + \gamma)}$}}\|\mathbf{\mu}\|_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big(-\exp\left(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(6+11\gamma+6\gamma^2+\gamma^3)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\right)+1\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \|\mu\|_2^2}{(4+\gamma)(2+\gamma)}$}}+o\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \|\mu\|_2^2}{(4+\gamma)(2+\gamma)}$}}\Big)+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma^3}$}}\right)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \|\mu\|_2^2}{(4+\gamma)(2+\gamma)}$}}+o\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma^2}$}}\Big)\;.

(34)

By performing Taylor expansions again, we can control
B_{1}
and
B_{3}
as

\displaystyle B_{1}\;=

\displaystyle\;1+\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{4+\gamma}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)\;=\;2+o(1)\;,

(35)

\displaystyle B_{3}\;=

\displaystyle\;1+\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2+\gamma}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)-4\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2(2+\gamma)}$}}\|\mu\|_{2}^{2}\Big)\;=\;-2+o(1)\;.

(36)

Substituting the bounds into (
30
) and using the bounds in (
31
) and (
32
), we obtain that

\displaystyle B\;=

\displaystyle\;(B_{1}-B_{2}-B_{3})+((1+\beta)^{d/2}-1)B_{1}+(1-(1-\alpha)^{d/2})B_{3}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \|\mu\|_2^2}{(4+\gamma)(2+\gamma)}$}}+o\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma^2}$}}\right)+\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3d}{2\gamma(4 + \gamma)}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{4} }$}}\right)\Big)\;(2+o(1))

\displaystyle\;+\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{2 (2 + \gamma)^2}$}}+O\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{ \gamma^{4} }$}}\right)\Big)(-2+o(1))

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \|\mu\|_2^2}{(4+\gamma)(2+\gamma)}$}}+o\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\bmu\|_2^2}{\gamma^2}$}}\right)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 d(6+4\gamma+\gamma^2)}{\gamma(2+\gamma)^2(4+\gamma)}$}}+o\left(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^2}$}}\right)\;=\;\Theta\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\|\mu\|_2^2}{\gamma^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{d}{\gamma^2}$}}\Big)\;.

The variance ratio can therefore be bounded as

\displaystyle\rho_{d}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigfull}{\sigcond}$}}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{B}}{\sqrt{A}}$}}\;=\;\Theta\Big(\,\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \gamma^{-2} \|\mu\|_2^2 + \gamma^{-2} d }{ \gamma^{-2} \|\mu\|_2^2 + \gamma^{-4} d\|\mu\|_2^4 }$}}\Big)^{1/2}\,\Big)\;=

\displaystyle\;\Theta\Big(\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ (\|\mu\|_2^2 + d)^{1/2} }{ ( \|\mu\|_2^2+ \gamma^{-2} d\|\mu\|_2^4)^{1/2} }$}}\,\Big)

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\Theta\Big(\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \|\mu\|_2 + d^{1/2} }{ \|\mu\|_2 + \gamma^{-1} d^{1/2} \|\mu\|_2^2 }$}}\,\Big)\;.

In
(a)
, we have noted that for
a,b>0
,
