and using the cyclic property of trace:

\displaystyle\text{\rm Var}[W_{n}^{K}]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n(n-1)}$}}\text{\rm Var}\big[(\eta^{K}_{1})^{\top}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\eta^{K}_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n(n-1)}$}}\text{\rm Tr}\big((\Lambda^{K}\Sigma^{K})^{2}\big)\;.

By Lemma
32
, we get the desired bound that

\displaystyle\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n(n-1)}$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}\;\leq\;\text{\rm Var}[W_{n}^{K}]\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n(n-1)}$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}+\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}\;.

The third central moment can be expanded using a binomial expansion and noting that each summand is zero-mean:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K}-D)^{3}\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^{3/2}(n-1)^{3/2}}$}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)\Big)^{3}\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^{3/2}(n-1)^{3/2}}$}}\mathbb{E}\big[\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{3}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)^{3}\big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{8}{n^{3/2}(n-1)^{3/2}}$}}\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{3}\;.

Meanwhile, the sum can be further expressed as

\displaystyle\;\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{3}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\text{\rm Tr}\Big(\big((\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\Lambda^{K}(\Sigma^{K})^{1/2}\big)^{3}\Big)\;=\;\text{\rm Tr}\Big(\big(\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K}\big)^{3}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\text{\rm Tr}\Big(\big(\mathbb{E}\big[\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{1})(\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{1}))^{\top}\big]\Lambda^{K}\big)^{3}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[(\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{1}))^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\,(\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{2}))^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{3})\,(\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{3}))^{\top}\Lambda^{K}\phi^{K}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)\big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{3})\big)\big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{3})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[S_{12}S_{23}S_{31}]\;.

We now approximate each
S_{ij}
term by
u(\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{X}_{j})
. For convenience, denote
U_{ij}=u(\mathbf{X}_{i},\mathbf{X}_{j})
and
\Delta_{ij}=S_{ij}-U_{ij}
. Then

\displaystyle\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{3}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[(U_{12}+\Delta_{12})(U_{23}+\Delta_{23})(U_{31}+\Delta_{31})\big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[U_{12}U_{23}U_{31}]+\mathbb{E}[U_{12}U_{23}\Delta_{31}]+\mathbb{E}[U_{12}\Delta_{23}U_{31}]+\mathbb{E}[U_{12}\Delta_{23}\Delta_{31}]

\displaystyle\;+\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{12}U_{23}U_{31}]+\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{12}U_{23}\Delta_{31}]+\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{12}\Delta_{23}U_{31}]+\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{12}\Delta_{23}\Delta_{31}]\;.

Recall that
\varepsilon_{K;3}=\mathbb{E}[|\Delta_{ij}|^{3}]^{1/3}
for
i\neq j
by definition. Then by a triangle inequality followed by a Hölder’s inequality, we get that

\displaystyle\;\Big|\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{3}-\mathbb{E}[u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})u(\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{3})u(\mathbf{X}_{3},\mathbf{X}_{1})]\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\big|\mathbb{E}[U_{12}U_{23}\Delta_{31}]\big|+\big|\mathbb{E}[U_{12}\Delta_{23}U_{31}]\big|+\big|\mathbb{E}[U_{12}\Delta_{23}\Delta_{31}]\big|

\displaystyle\;+\big|\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{12}U_{23}U_{31}]\big|+\big|\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{12}U_{23}\Delta_{31}]\big|+\big|\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{12}\Delta_{23}U_{31}]\big|+\big|\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{12}\Delta_{23}\Delta_{31}]\big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;3\mathbb{E}[|u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})|^{3}]^{2/3}\varepsilon_{K;3}+3\mathbb{E}[|u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})|^{3}]^{1/3}\varepsilon_{K;3}^{2}+\varepsilon_{K;3}^{3}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;3M_{\rm full;3}^{2}\varepsilon_{K;3}+3M_{\rm full;3}\varepsilon_{K;3}^{2}+\varepsilon_{K;3}^{3}\;.

This implies that

\displaystyle\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{3}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})u(\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{3})u(\mathbf{X}_{3},\mathbf{X}_{1})]-M_{\rm full;3}^{3}+(M_{\rm full;3}+\varepsilon_{K;3})^{3}\;,

\displaystyle\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{3}\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})u(\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{3})u(\mathbf{X}_{3},\mathbf{X}_{1})]+M_{\rm full;3}^{3}-(M_{\rm full;3}+\varepsilon_{K;3})^{3}\;,

which gives the desired bounds:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K}-D)^{3}\big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{8 \big( \mean[u(\bX_1,\bX_2)u(\bX_2,\bX_3)u(\bX_3,\bX_1)] - \Mfullthree^3 + ( \Mfullthree+ \varepsilon_{K;3})^3 \big)}{n^{3/2}(n-1)^{3/2}}$}}\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K}-D)^{3}\big]\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{8 \big( \mean[u(\bX_1,\bX_2)u(\bX_2,\bX_3)u(\bX_3,\bX_1)] + \Mfullthree^3 - ( \Mfullthree+ \varepsilon_{K;3})^3 \big)}{n^{3/2}(n-1)^{3/2}}$}}\;.

The fourth central moment can again be expanded using a binomial expansion and noting that each summand is zero-mean:

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K}-D)^{4}\big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^2(n-1)^2}$}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)\Big)^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^2(n-1)^2}$}}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{4}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)^{4}\Big]+3\mathbb{E}\Big[\msum_{1\leq k\neq k^{\prime}\leq K}\tau_{k;d}^{2}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)^{2}\tau_{k^{\prime};d}^{2}(\xi_{k^{\prime}}^{2}-1)^{2}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^2(n-1)^2}$}}\Big(60\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{4}+12\msum_{1\leq k\neq k^{\prime}\leq K}\tau_{k;d}^{2}\tau_{k^{\prime};d}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^2(n-1)^2}$}}\Big(48\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{4}+12\msum_{1\leq k,k^{\prime}\leq K}\tau_{k;d}^{2}\tau_{k^{\prime};d}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{12}{n^2(n-1)^2}$}}\Big(4\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{4}+\big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{2}\big)^{2}\Big)\;.

Since we have already controlled
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{2}=\text{\rm Tr}\big((\Sigma^{K}\Lambda^{K})^{2}\big)
, we focus on bounding the first sum. Using notations from the third moment, we can express the sum as

\displaystyle\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{4}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\big)\big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{2})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{3})\big)

\displaystyle\;\quad\;\big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{3})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{4})\big)\big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\lambda_{k}\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{4})\phi_{k}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[S_{12}S_{23}S_{34}S_{41}]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[(U_{12}+\Delta_{12})(U_{23}+\Delta_{23})(U_{34}+\Delta_{34})(U_{41}+\Delta_{41})\big]\;.

A similar argument as before shows that

\displaystyle\Big|\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{4}-\mathbb{E}[u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})u(\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{3})

\displaystyle u(\mathbf{X}_{3},\mathbf{X}_{4})u(\mathbf{X}_{4},\mathbf{X}_{1})]\Big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;4M_{\rm full;4}^{3}\varepsilon_{K;4}+6M_{\rm full;4}^{2}\varepsilon_{K;4}^{2}+4M_{\rm full;4}\varepsilon_{K;4}^{3}+\varepsilon_{K;4}^{4}\;.

This implies that

\displaystyle\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{4}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})u(\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{3})u(\mathbf{X}_{3},\mathbf{X}_{4})u(\mathbf{X}_{4},\mathbf{X}_{1})]-M_{\rm full;4}^{4}+(M_{\rm full;4}+\varepsilon_{K;4})^{4}\;,

\displaystyle\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{4}\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})u(\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{3})u(\mathbf{X}_{3},\mathbf{X}_{4})u(\mathbf{X}_{4},\mathbf{X}_{1})]+M_{\rm full;4}^{4}-(M_{\rm full;4}+\varepsilon_{K;4})^{4}\;.

On the other hand, by Lemma
32
, we have

\displaystyle(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}\;\leq\;\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{2}\;=\;\text{\rm Tr}((\Lambda^{K}\Sigma^{K})^{2})\;\leq\;(\sigma_{\rm full}+\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2}\;.

Combining the results give the desired bounds:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K}-D)^{4}\big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{12}{n^2(n-1)^2}$}}\Big(4\,\mathbb{E}[u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})u(\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{3})u(\mathbf{X}_{3},\mathbf{X}_{4})u(\mathbf{X}_{4},\mathbf{X}_{1})]

\displaystyle\;\qquad\qquad\quad-4M_{\rm full;4}^{4}+4(M_{\rm full;4}+\varepsilon_{K;4})^{4}+(\sigma_{\rm full}+\varepsilon_{K;2})^{4}\Big)\;,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K}-D)^{4}\big]\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{12}{n^2(n-1)^2}$}}\Big(4\,\mathbb{E}[u(\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2})u(\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{3})u(\mathbf{X}_{3},\mathbf{X}_{4})u(\mathbf{X}_{4},\mathbf{X}_{1})]

\displaystyle\;\qquad\qquad\quad+4M_{\rm full;4}^{4}-4(M_{\rm full;4}+\varepsilon_{K;4})^{4}+(\sigma_{\rm full}-\varepsilon_{K;2})^{4}\Big)\;.

For the generic moment bound, we first use a Jensen’s inequality to get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K})^{2m}\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^{1/2}(n-1)^{1/2}}$}}\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)+D\Big)^{2m}\Big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2^{2m-1}}{n^{m}(n-1)^{m} }$}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)\Big)^{2m}\Big]+2^{2m-1}\,D^{2m}\;.

Denote the set of all possible orderings of a length-
2m
sequence consisting of elements from
[K]
by
\mathcal{P}(K,2m)
and denote its elements by
p
. Consider the subset

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(K,2m)\;\coloneqq\;\{p\in\mathcal{P}(K,2m)\,:\,\text{ every element in }p\text{ appears at least twice }\}\;.

By noting that
\xi_{k}-1
is zero-mean and
\{\xi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{K}
are independent, we can re-express the sum first as a sum over
\mathcal{P}(K,2m)
and then as a sum over
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(K,2m)
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)\Big)^{2m}\Big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{p\in\mathcal{P}(K,2m)}\big(\mprod_{k\in p}\tau_{k;d}\big)\mathbb{E}\big[\mprod_{k\in p}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)\big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{p\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(K,2m)}\big(\mprod_{k\in p}\tau_{k;d}\big)\mathbb{E}\big[\mprod_{k\in p}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)\big]

\displaystyle\;+\msum_{p\in\big(\mathcal{P}(K,2m)\setminus\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(K,2m)\big)}\big(\mprod_{k\in p}\tau_{k;d}\big)\mathbb{E}\big[\mprod_{k\in p}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)\big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\msum_{p\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(K,2m)}\big(\mprod_{k\in p}\tau_{k;d}\big)\mathbb{E}\big[\mprod_{k\in p}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)\big]\;.

Write
C^{\prime}_{m}
as the
2m
-th central moment of a chi-squared random variable with degree
1
, which depends only on
m
and not on
K
or
\tau_{k;d}
. By a Hölder’s inequality and the bound from Lemma
32
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}(\xi_{k}^{2}-1)\Big)^{2m}\Big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}_{m}\,\msum_{p\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(K,2m)}\big(\mprod_{k\in p}\tau_{k;d}\big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}_{m}\,\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{2m}{m}$}}\Bigr)\big(\msum_{k=1}^{K}\tau_{k;d}^{2}\big)^{m}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;C^{\prime}_{m}\,\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{2m}{m}$}}\Bigr)\,\text{\rm Tr}\big((\Lambda^{K}\Sigma^{K})^{2}\big)^{m}\;\leq\;C^{\prime}_{m}\,\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{2m}{m}$}}\Bigr)\,(\sigma_{\rm full}+\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2m}\;.

Writing
C_{m}\coloneqq 2^{2m-1}\max\{1,C^{\prime}_{m}\,\binom{2m}{m}\}
, we get the desired bound that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K})^{2m}\big]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C_m}{n^{m}(n-1)^{m} }$}}(\sigma_{\rm full}+\varepsilon_{K;2})^{2m}+C_{m}\,D^{2m}\;.

Finally, if
2
is true for some
\nu\geq 2
, we have
\varepsilon_{K;2}\rightarrow 0
as
K
grows. Taking
K\rightarrow\infty
in the bound for second moment gives

\displaystyle\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\text{\rm Var}[W_{n}^{K}]\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{n(n-1)}$}}\sigma_{\rm full}^{2}\;.

If
2
holds for
\nu\geq 3
, similarly we have

\displaystyle\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K}-D)^{3}\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{8 \mean[u(\bX_1,\bX_2)u(\bX_2,\bX_3)u(\bX_3,\bX_1)]}{n^{3/2}(n-1)^{3/2}}$}}\;.

If
2
holds for
\nu\geq 4
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{K\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[(W_{n}^{K}-D)^{4}\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{12 (4 \mean[u(\bX_1,\bX_2)u(\bX_2,\bX_3)u(\bX_3,\bX_4)u(\bX_4,\bX_1)] + \sigfull^4)}{n^2(n-1)^2}$}}\;.

\jmlrQED

G.3
Proofs for
Section

B.3

Proof of Lemma
34

Write
\delta^{\prime}:=\delta/(m+1)
for convenience. Define the
m
-times differentiable function

\displaystyle h_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)\;\coloneqq\;(\delta^{\prime})^{-(m+1)}\mint_{x}^{x+\delta^{\prime}}\mint_{y_{1}}^{y_{1}+\delta^{\prime}}\ldots\mint_{y_{m-1}}^{y_{m-1}+\delta^{\prime}}\mint_{y_{m}}^{y_{m}+\delta^{\prime}}\mathbb{I}_{\{y>\tau\}}\;dy\,dy_{m}\ldots dy_{1}\;.

In the case
m=0
, the function is
h_{0;\tau;\delta}(x)\;\coloneqq\;\delta^{-1}\int_{x}^{x+\delta}\mathbb{I}_{\{y>\tau\}}\,dy
. By construction,
h_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)=0
for
x\leq\tau-\delta
,
h_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)\in[0,1]
for
x\in(\tau-\delta,\tau]
and
h_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)=1
for
x>\tau
. This implies
\mathbb{I}_{\{x>\tau\}}\leq h_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)\leq\mathbb{I}_{\{x>\tau-\delta\}}
and therefore the desired inequality

h_{m;\tau+\delta;\delta}(x)\;\leq\;\mathbb{I}_{\{x>\tau\}}\;\leq\;h_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)\;.

Next, we prove the properties of the derivatives of
h_{m;\tau;\delta}
. Denote recursively

\displaystyle J_{m+1}(x)\;:=\;\mint_{x}^{x+\delta^{\prime}}\mathbb{I}_{\{y>\tau\}}dy\;,

\displaystyle J_{r}(x)\;:=\;\mint_{x}^{x+\delta^{\prime}}J_{r+1}(y)\,dy\;\;\text{ for }\;0\leq r\leq m\;.

Since
h_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)=(\delta^{\prime})^{-(m+1)}J_{0}(x)
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial x}J_{i}(x)=J_{i+1}(x+\delta^{\prime})-J_{i+1}(x)
for
0\leq i\leq m
, by induction, we have that for
0\leq r\leq m
,

\displaystyle h^{(r)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)\;=

\displaystyle\;(\delta^{\prime})^{-(m+1)}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\partial^{r}}{\partial x^r}$}}J_{0}(x)\;=\;(\delta^{\prime})^{-(m+1)}\msum_{i=0}^{r}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{r}{i}$}}\Bigr)(-1)^{i}\,J_{r+1}\big(x+(r-i)\delta^{\prime}\big)\;.

(44)

Note that
J_{m+1}
is continuous, uniformly bounded above by
\delta^{\prime}
, and satisfies that
J_{m+1}(x)=0
for
x
outside
[\tau-\delta^{\prime},\tau]
. By induction, we get that for
0\leq r\leq m
,
J_{r+1}
is continuous, bounded above by
(\delta^{\prime})^{m+1-r}
and satisfies that
J_{r+1}(x)=0
for
x
outside
[\tau-(m+1-r)\delta^{\prime},\tau]
. This shows that
h^{(r)}_{m;\tau;\delta}
is continuous and
h^{(r)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)=0
for
x
outside
[\tau-\delta,\tau]
, and the uniform bound

\displaystyle\big|h^{(r)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)\big|\;\leq\;(\delta^{\prime})^{-r}\msum_{i=0}^{r}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{r}{i}$}}\Bigr)\;=\;\big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2}{m+1}$}}\big)^{r}\delta^{-r}\;\leq\;\delta^{-r}\;.

Finally to prove the Hölder property of
h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)
, we first note that
J_{m+1}
is constant outside
x\in[\tau-\delta^{\prime},\tau]
and linear within the interval with Lipschitz constant
1
. The formula in (
44
) suggests that
h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)
is piecewise linear and the Lipschitz constant in the interval
[\tau-(m-i+1)\delta^{\prime},\tau-(m-i)\delta^{\prime}]
is given by the Lipschitz constant of the
i
-th summand. Therefore,
h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}
is also Lipschitz with Lipschitz constant

\displaystyle L_{m}\;\coloneqq\;(\delta^{\prime})^{-(m+1)}\max\nolimits_{0\leq i\leq m}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{i}$}}\Bigr)\;=\;(\delta^{\prime})^{-(m+1)}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{\lfloor m/2 \rfloor}$}}\Bigr)\;.

For
x,y\in[\tau-\delta,\tau]
, we then have

\displaystyle|h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)-h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(y)|\;\leq\;L_{m}|x-y|\;=

\displaystyle\;L_{m}\delta\,\big|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{x-y}{\delta}$}}\big|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;L_{m}\delta\,\big|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{x-y}{\delta}$}}\big|^{\epsilon}\;=\;L_{m}\delta^{1-\epsilon}|x-y|^{\epsilon}\;,

(45)

where we have noted that
\big|\frac{x-y}{\delta}\big|\leq 1
and
\epsilon\in[0,1]
. (
45
) is trivially true for
x,y
both outside
[\tau-\delta,\tau]
since
h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}
evaluates to zero. Now consider
x\in[\tau-\delta,\tau]
and
y<\tau-\delta
. We have that

\displaystyle|h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)-h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(y)|\;=\;|h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)-h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(\tau-\delta)|\;\overset{\eqref{eqn:h:m:lipschitz}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;L_{m}\delta^{1-\epsilon}(x-\tau+\delta)^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;L_{m}\delta^{1-\epsilon}|x-y|^{\epsilon}\;.

Similarly for
x\in[\tau-\delta,\tau]
and
y>\tau
, we have that

\displaystyle|h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)-h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(y)|\;=\;|h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)-h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(\tau)|\;\overset{\eqref{eqn:h:m:lipschitz}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;L_{m}\delta^{1-\epsilon}(\tau-x)^{\epsilon}\;\leq\;L_{m}\delta^{1-\epsilon}|x-y|^{\epsilon}\;.

Therefore (
45
) holds for all
x,y
. The proof for the derivative bound is complete by computing the constant explicitly as

\displaystyle L_{m}\delta^{1-\epsilon}\;=\;(\delta^{\prime})^{-(m+\epsilon)}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{\lfloor m/2 \rfloor}$}}\Bigr)\;=\;\delta^{-(m+\epsilon)}\Bigl(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{m}{\lfloor m/2 \rfloor}$}}\Bigr)(m+1)^{m+\epsilon}\;,

and therefore

\displaystyle|h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(x)-h^{(m)}_{m;\tau;\delta}(y)|\;\leq\;C_{m,\epsilon}\,\delta^{-(m+\epsilon)}\,|x-y|^{\epsilon}\;,

(46)

with respect to the constant
C_{m,\epsilon}=\binom{m}{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}(m+1)^{m+\epsilon}
.

\jmlrQED

Proof of Lemma
35

By conditioning on the size of
Y
, we have that for any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq X+Y\leq b)\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}(a\leq X+Y\leq b\,,\,|Y|\leq\epsilon)+\mathbb{P}(a\leq X\leq b\,,\,|Y|\geq\epsilon)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}(a-\epsilon\leq X\leq b+\epsilon)+\mathbb{P}(|Y|\geq\epsilon)\;,

and by using the order of inclusion of events, we have the lower bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(a\leq X+Y\leq b)\;\geq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}(a+\epsilon\leq X\leq b-\epsilon\,,\,|Y|\leq\epsilon)

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{P}(a+\epsilon\leq X\leq b-\epsilon)-\mathbb{P}(|Y|\geq\epsilon)\;.

\jmlrQED

G.4
Proof for
Section

B.4

Proof of Lemma
37

By Lemma 2.3 of
Steinwart and Scovel 2012
, the assumption that
\kappa^{*}
is measurable and
\mathbb{E}[\kappa^{*}(\mathbf{V}_{1},\mathbf{V}_{1})]<\infty
implies the RKHS
\mathcal{H}
associated with
\kappa^{*}
is compactly embedded into
L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},R)
. By Lemma 2.12 and Corollary 3.2 of
Steinwart and Scovel 2012
, for some index set
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbb{N}
, there exists a sequence of non-negative, bounded values
\{\lambda_{k}\}_{k\in\mathcal{I}}
that converges to
0
and a sequence of functions
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k\in\mathcal{I}}
that form an orthonormal basis of
L_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d},R)
such that

\displaystyle\msum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\lambda_{k}\psi_{k}(\mathbf{V}_{1})\psi_{k}(\mathbf{V}_{2})\;=\;\kappa^{*}(\mathbf{V}_{1},\mathbf{V}_{2})\;,

where the equality holds almost surely when
\mathcal{I}
is finite and the convergence holds almost surely when
\mathcal{I}
is infinite. We can extend
\mathcal{I}
to
\mathbb{N}
by adding zero values of
\lambda_{k}
and
\phi_{k}
whenever necessary and drop the requirement that
\{\phi_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
forms a basis, which gives the desired statement.

\jmlrQED
